# Decisions & Open Threads

Append-only log of ideas raised, decisions made, and threads in flight. Move entries between sections; don't delete history.

## How to use

Three sections:
- **Open** — questions or ideas awaiting a decision or action
- **Decided** — resolved entries with outcome
- **Archived** — things that became irrelevant or superseded

Entry format:

```
### YYYY-MM-DD — Short title
- **Context:** what came up
- **Options:** A / B / C (brief tradeoffs)
- **Status / Decision:** where things stand
- **Outcome / Commit:** hash + phase if implemented
```

When starting a session, scan **Open** first. Surface anything that's been pending too long.

---

## Open

### 2026-08-23 - macOS: the site's JS is dead in the in-app Browser (diagnostic build)
- **Symptom.** On macOS the workspace Browser loads a page and renders HTML/CSS,
  but the site's own JavaScript never runs. Windows is fine, and
  `workspace_browser.rs` has no `cfg(target_os)` arms at all - every line of it
  was written against WebView2 and runs unchanged under WKWebView.
- **Ruled out before writing any code.** Entitlements: `Entitlements.plist`
  already carries `allow-jit` + `allow-unsigned-executable-memory` (they landed
  in the same commit as `hardenedRuntime`, `048f1b2`), and the app's MAIN window
  is itself a WKWebView that renders - so JavaScriptCore works in-process. CSP:
  `tauri.conf.json` sets `"csp": null`, and Tauri's CSP does not apply to an
  external URL regardless. `javascript_disabled` defaults to `false` in
  tauri-runtime, so it is not a Tauri toggle.
- **What shipped is a DIAGNOSTIC, not a fix.** Rule #17 means no local mac build,
  so the probe is baked into the binary: `browser_diag.js` runs at document start
  and reports through `document.title` -> `on_document_title_changed`, plus an
  `on_page_load` line and a Rust-side `eval` probe that together separate three
  hypotheses in one launch (engine dead / site scripts never executed - including
  an ATS block on cross-origin `http://` script sources / a WebKit-vs-Chromium
  SyntaxError that is not our bug at all).
- **Why `document.title` and not the existing `ymux-ticket:` navigation bridge.**
  Read out of wry 0.54.4: `fetch`/`<img>` cannot reach `on_navigation` (it is the
  `decidePolicyForNavigationAction` delegate - frame navigations only); an iframe
  beacon works on mac but is silently dead on Windows (`webview2/mod.rs` hooks
  `NavigationStarting`, never `FrameNavigationStarting`), which would destroy the
  Windows control run; and `location.href` is a main-frame navigation fired in
  the exact window we are trying to measure. Title change is the one hook wry
  implements symmetrically on both platforms.
- **The probe is NOT cfg-gated to macOS, deliberately.** If it first executed on
  the one machine we cannot debug, "no beacon in the log" would be ambiguous
  between "the JS engine is dead" and "my probe is broken". Windows runs it first
  as the control.
- **DECIDED (Yossi, 2026-08-23) - DevTools stays, scoped to the Browser webview,
  on BOTH platforms, with a button.** He asked for it directly: "so we can see
  everything including errors." The `.devtools(cfg!(target_os = "macos"))` that
  shipped first is now `.devtools(true)` - a Windows control run you cannot
  inspect is worth much less than one you can - plus a
  `workspace_browser_open_devtools` command and a terminal-icon button next to
  the Dev Mode toggle in the Browser header. That is the only entry point that
  exists on Windows (F12 is not wired up for a child webview) and it saves a
  trip through Safari -> Develop -> machine -> webview on macOS.
  - **The main window and popouts stay OUT, and this is the line to hold.**
    Enabling the `devtools` feature makes tauri-runtime-wry read
    `devtools.unwrap_or(true)`, so every webview is inspectable unless it says
    otherwise. The two explicit `.devtools(false)` calls in lib.rs are what keep
    ymux's own UI - which renders live PTY output - out of an inspector, and the
    deliberate F12 / Ctrl+Shift+I blocker at `App.tsx:3184` stays. Anyone adding
    a webview later inherits `true` by default: opt it out at the builder.
  - Scope justification: the Browser child webview shows a tunneled third-party
    service, not ymux's own surface, so an inspector attached there exposes
    nothing of ours. On macOS `isInspectable` only means the app shows up in
    Safari's Develop menu on the same machine, for a user who already enabled
    that menu - it is not a remote surface.
- **Also corrected on the way.** The comment at `workspace_browser.rs` claiming no
  Tauri IPC is injected into the child webview is factually wrong for 2.10.3 -
  `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` is prepended to every webview including external URLs.
  What actually denies the tunneled page is the capability layer (`Origin::Remote`
  vs the `Local` execution context every capability declares). Left as a comment
  because `capabilities/default.json` is scoped to `windows: ["main"]` and this
  webview lives in the `main` window - a `remote` context added there would expose
  it.
### 2026-08-23 — TMUX organization: workspace scope, and "attach means attach"
- **Context:** Two complaints, one root. (1) With project folders now being child workspaces with their own `cwd`, the tmux picker still listed every session on the host — `pane_list_claude_sessions` had been folder-scoped since v4 and the tmux list next to it in the same wizard never was. (2) Opening the connect wizard on a pane already mounted on a tmux session restarted it, or typed the chosen command into whatever it was running.
- **The mechanism for (2), for the record:** `openNewConnModal` hard-set `ncType("tmux")`; `submitNewConn` sent `persistent:true` + `mode:"claude"`; `build_tmux_attach_script` runs `new-session -A -s <name>` — attach-OR-create — so the pane JOINED the live session at T+900ms, and the smart-connect injection typed `cd … && claude --resume …\r\n` into its foreground at T+1100ms. No `$TMUX` check existed anywhere in the codebase, and the only guard was a comment on `pickTmuxSession` saying it injects nothing.
- **Decided (Yossi's calls):**
  - **Scope is BOTH directory and ownership**, not either. tmux answers `#{session_path}` so the cwd is free — but `zellij list-sessions` reports no directory at all, so on Windows a cwd-only scope would match nothing. `session-owners.json` (`%APPDATA%\ymux`, keyed host → session → workspace) is the half that covers zellij, and it also covers a tmux session whose cwd has moved.
  - **A toggle in the picker, not a filter and not a setting.** The backend ANNOTATES every row with `owned`/`in_cwd` and filters nothing, so flipping the toggle is a client-side view change with no second round trip, and a session outside the scope stays one click away. A count line ("N of M on the host") is what stops a scoped list from reading as an empty server.
  - **A session that already exists gets NOTHING typed into it** — not the command, and not the `cd` either. `cd /srv/app` landing in a live `claude` is the same bug with a shorter payload. The user's choice is not silently dropped: the wizard warns before the command is picked, the command controls grey out, a toast fires if it happens anyway, and "Start a new session instead" makes `<name>-2` so the command can run for real.
- **The one place the guard is deliberately weak, and why.** An SSH workspace with no live handle cannot be asked. Treating "cannot ask" as "might be live" would silently drop the command on every FIRST connect to an SSH workspace — where there is no handle by definition — trading a real bug for a worse one. So a DERIVED name on an unreachable host falls back to today's behaviour, while an EXPLICIT picker name is always treated as live (the picker only ever lists sessions that exist). The frontend closes the gap for the path a human actually takes. FOLLOWUPS P3.
- **`project_path` is optional and `null` means unscoped, which is load-bearing.** Session restore and `pane_probe_tmux_sessions` share these list paths and must see everything: a pane whose session sits outside the current folder still has to come back on the next boot. Both call sites pass `projectPath: null` explicitly rather than relying on the default.
- **Found on the way, fixed rather than filed:** the `is_local → list_zellij_sessions()` arm was never `cfg`-gated, in `pane_list_tmux_sessions` AND in `pane_probe_tmux_sessions`. Both return unconditionally for a local workspace, so they shadowed the `cfg(not(windows))` macOS branches below them — a Mac asked zellij, got nothing, and never reached its own tmux server. The new picker is built on that call and would have been silently empty on macOS, so this was a prerequisite, not a drive-by. FOLLOWUPS P2.
- **Also removed:** 14 dead `tmux_picker.*` i18n keys per language (56 total), left behind by a picker UI deleted long ago, with zero references in `app/src`.
- **Status:** Open — compiles-untested until CI, and Rule #14 until the smoke list in FOLLOWUPS (P1) runs live.

#### Addendum, 2026-08-24 — the "Whole server" view says whose a session is
- **Context.** The toggle above shipped with a flat server list: a session free to take and a session already mounted on another project rendered identically. Yossi: *"אני רוצה שנוסיף במסך של כל השרת — מה שאנחנו יודעים ששייך לתיקייה אחרת כדי למנוע בלאגנים."* The information was already on the machine and being discarded — `annotate_session_scope` computed `owned` as `o.workspace_id == workspace_id` and dropped the id when it did not match.
- **Decided (Yossi's calls, asked before building):**
  - **A badge, not a confirm dialog and not a re-sort.** It marks; it does not block. Attaching to another folder's session is allowed and is sometimes exactly what you want — the failure mode being fixed is doing it *without noticing*, not doing it at all.
  - **A session we cannot place shows NOTHING.** Not a muted "unknown folder" chip. This is the 2026-08-23 unknown-`cwd` rule applied to the UI: every zellij session and every five-field tmux would wear that chip permanently, which trains the eye to ignore it and makes the real badge worthless.
- **The invariant that removed a frontend conditional.** `foreign` is never `Some` while `owned || in_cwd`, so the picker's "This folder" view is *defined* as the complement of the field and the badge cannot appear there by construction. That also settles the two-workspaces-on-one-folder case: both belong there, and a warning would be false. Asserted in `a_foreign_badge_is_impossible_inside_the_workspace_view`.
- **One field carrying an object, not two flat ones.** `foreign: Option<ForeignScope>` = `{ kind, label?, path? }`. Flat `Option`s would admit four states of which only three are legal. Three facts are genuinely needed: `path` because on Windows — where this matters most — zellij reports no `cwd` at all, so the claim's recorded folder is the only path there is; `kind` because "belongs to workspace X" and "runs in folder Y" are different warnings and every string is i18n'd in four languages, so the backend cannot ship the sentence; and `label: None` because nothing prunes `session-owners.json` when a workspace is deleted, so a stale row can be *known foreign and unnameable* — a state a bare `Option<String>` could only express with a magic empty string.
- **`annotate_session_scope` was split, and that is the point.** The disk read moved out to a shim; `annotate_scope_with` takes the owners map as an argument. The 2026-08-23 logic had no unit tests because it did file I/O inline — this is the change that made the scope rules testable, and eight tests landed with it.
### 2026-08-23 — Fork scan: what the four real forks of this repo actually added
- **Context:** Yossi asked whether any of the people who forked the repo added something worth adopting. Scanned everything reachable: 4 forks that share our history (`netanelhibsh/ymux`, `bouhady/winmux`, `Efrat-Cohen/winmux`, `sh770/ymux`) plus the same-name repos that turned up in the search and are **not** ours (`jozefkun/ymux`, `kwanmo2/ymux`, and the whole `winmux` name-collision family from COMPETITIVE-SCAN — `stoneyang0213/winmux` has no common ancestor with us at all). Method: fetched each fork and listed `git rev-list <branch> --not --remotes=origin`, so branches that merely mirror ours were excluded rather than counted.
- **What is genuinely fork-original (everything else is our own branches, mirrored):**
  - **`bouhady/winmux` — a working Android port. 13 commits, ~13.8k lines, `mobile/` (Phases 82.A–82.K, 2026-08-09).** Standalone Tauri v2 Android SSH client with its own Cargo + npm workspace: SSH connect, on-device ed25519 keygen (no `ssh-keygen` on Android), TOFU host keys, tmux session picker, xterm.js terminal, `build-android.yml` publishing an APK. Note: it is 189 commits behind, pre-rename (`winmux-*` crate names), and its Phase 82 number **collides with our Phase 82** (the macOS unification).
  - **`Efrat-Cohen/winmux` — 3 CI commits (2026-07-28).** Largely superseded by today's `build-windows.yml` + `ci-windows.yml`, with one idea we do not have: split the Linux-CLI cross-compile into a **native Linux musl job** and hand `ymux-linux-x64` to the Windows job as an artifact, with `.github/tauri.ci.conf.json` swapping `beforeBuildCommand` for a plain `vite build`. Also runs the frontend node tests (`node --experimental-strip-types --test src/*.test.ts`), which our CI does not.
  - **`netanelhibsh/ymux` — 1 commit, `build-macos-arm64.yml` (2026-08-23).** A copy of `build-macos-intel.yml` with the triple swapped. **Superseded before it landed** by our own unmerged `claude/macos-universal-signing` (048f1b2, 2026-08-21), which does the same thing as a two-leg matrix plus Developer ID signing and per-arch `lipo` assertions. Worth reading as a signal, not as a patch: a second person hit "no Apple Silicon build" within two days.
  - **`sh770/ymux`** — a clean snapshot of our `main` at `3616b30`. Nothing added.
- **The one thing worth taking on its own merits (from 82.A):** `try_authenticate`'s step 3 resolves the home dir with `env::var("USERPROFILE").or_else(HOME).map_err(…)?`, so a missing home dir aborts the **whole auth ladder before password auth**, not just the step that needs it. Still present on `main` at `crates/ymux-ssh/src/lib.rs:339`. Logged in FOLLOWUPS (P3 — no shipping target of ours leaves both vars unset, so it is correctness, not a live bug).
- **Options for the Android port:** (A) ignore it; (B) cherry-pick only the shared-crate groundwork (82.A `pty` feature on `ymux-core` + the auth-ladder fix, 82.B the `ymux-tmux` extraction) and drop `mobile/`; (C) rebase the whole port onto `main` and adopt it as a second product. (C) is a real project: 189 commits of drift, the rename, and a third platform to keep green in CI — and CLAUDE.md already records what one platform fork cost us in two months.
- **Status:** Yossi's call. Nothing adopted yet; no fork code has been merged.

### 2026-08-18 — Project folders v4: a project folder IS a workspace (SUPERSEDES v2 and v1)
- **What changed and why.** v2 and v3 were both rejected on sight for the same reason, which took four rounds to hear correctly. Yossi: *"it pins the whole workspace to a folder inside it, instead of opening a **sub-workspace** of that folder — and then it would let you open panes in that environment, directly on that folder."* The `ProjectFolder` entity is gone. What it modelled — a directory on a host you keep coming back to — is a workspace with a `cwd`; the only field genuinely missing from the schema was a parent.
- **Decided (Yossi's calls, 2026-08-18):**
  - Pinning creates a **full child workspace**: own panes, own layout, `cwd` = the folder, connection cloned from the parent. Worktrees list beneath it; each opens another child.
  - The folder appears **only nested under its parent**, and **deleting the parent deletes it**. This directly supersedes the 2026-08-16 entry below, which said unpinning must destroy nothing — that was right when the row was a *section* and its workspaces outlived it. Now the row IS the workspace.
  - A new pane inside such a workspace gets a **mini wizard**: TMUX/regular toggle, the existing command list, and a Claude-session list **scoped to that folder**.
- **`is_project_root` is an explicit bit, not derived.** Every derivation fails: "has a parent" catches worktree children, and `git worktree list` from a linked worktree returns the same list *including main*, so scanning one duplicates the whole subtree under itself; "has children" is false for a fresh pin, which is exactly when the list must appear; "cwd is a repo" is an SSH round-trip per row per render, against the rule that scans are lazy.
- **`worktree_path` was dropped, not kept.** A worktree child's `cwd` IS its worktree path. Two fields that must agree eventually disagree; the user editing `cwd` unbinds the row and re-shows a stub, which is arguably correct and definitely not destructive.
- **The cascade delete is the one real cost of this model.** Deleting a workspace now kills the remote sessions of every folder and worktree under it. Directories on the host are untouched. The confirm names each descendant and how many are live — it is the only thing standing between a right-click and that.
- **v3 pins are migrated, not dropped.** See docs/CONFIG.md. A pin the user made is data; discarding it because the schema moved would have been a choice, not a constraint.
- **Session scoping is a correctness fix, not a filter.** Per the current Claude Code docs, `--resume <id>` searches the current project directory and its git worktrees, *then every other project on the machine*. An unscoped list does not fail on a foreign pick — it succeeds, resuming the wrong repo's conversation inside your folder.
- **Two pre-existing bugs found on the way, both wider than this feature:** (1) `list_claude_sessions_local` never got Phase 65's fix and still reported the *encoded* directory name as `project_path`, invisible only because the caller's guard is `startsWith("/")`; (2) `spawn_wsl_pty` guards `--cd` with a Windows-side `is_dir()` check that a Linux path always fails, so WSL silently ignored a workspace `cwd`. Both fixed here rather than deferred.
- **Still open:** nothing in v4 has run live. Smoke list in FOLLOWUPS (P1, 2026-08-18).


### 2026-08-16 — Project folders v2 (SUPERSEDED by v4 above): a worktree IS a workspace (cmux model)
- **What changed and why.** The v1 design below was built and rejected on sight. It hung the folder off a workspace as metadata and made "open session here" a *pane* in that same workspace. Yossi: *"it pins the whole workspace, I wanted a sub-workspace like in CMUX — shown underneath."* His **first** message had already said "like the groups logic" — a group is a collapsible section whose children are workspaces — and that was read as "looks like a group" instead of "is shaped like a group". The lesson is cheap to state and was expensive here: when a user names an existing UI concept, they are naming the *structure*, not the *appearance*.
- **What cmux actually does** (looked up, not recalled): the sidebar has switchable views, one being **Project Worktrees** ([#5173](https://github.com/manaflow-ai/cmux/issues/5173)); a project row's `+` "creates both the git worktree on disk and opens a workspace" ([#5032](https://github.com/manaflow-ai/cmux/issues/5032)). A worktree materializes as a workspace. Nesting a workspace's *tabs* under it is requested but unshipped there ([#1404](https://github.com/manaflow-ai/cmux/discussions/1404)) and we did not copy it.
- **Decided:**
  - `ProjectFolder` lives on `WorkspacesFile`, beside `groups`, and **owns its `Connection`**. Rejected: keeping it on a workspace. A folder that borrows a workspace's connection can't be pinned until some workspace happens to point at that host, which is backwards — the repo is the durable thing, the workspace is the disposable one.
  - **Unpin destroys nothing.** Not the directory (winmux never made it) and not the workspaces opened from it — those are real sessions. They keep `worktree_path` and fall back to the flat list, so re-pinning re-adopts them.
  - **SSH handle lookup is by `user@host:port`, not workspace id.** A folder belongs to a host; once any pane is connected there, refusing to list worktrees would be arbitrary. `key_path` is a credential, not an identity, so it is not matched.
  - Stole cmux#5032's lesson: create the worktree **first**, then open a workspace whose main process is an interactive shell. They spawned it with the setup command as PID 1 and the tab died when the command exited.
- **Bug found on the way, wider than this feature: `Workspace.cwd` is inert over SSH.** `pane_connect`'s `Ssh` arm never forwards `cwd` to `spawn_ssh` (only the Local and WSL arms use it), so every SSH pane has always started in `$HOME` and a hand-set workspace cwd was silently dropped. An SSH channel has no working-directory parameter — that is precisely what `cwdOverride` exists to paper over, via the `cd '<dir>'` in `build_smart_connect_script`. `connectPane` now defaults the override to the workspace's cwd for remote workspaces. Fixed generally rather than narrowed to worktrees: the narrow version would have left every other SSH cwd still broken.
- **Still open:** none of v2 has run live either. Same smoke list as below, plus: unpin a folder and confirm its workspaces survive in the flat list, and re-pin and confirm they are re-adopted.

### 2026-08-13 — Project folders v1 (SUPERSEDED, see above)
- **Context:** Yossi wanted a way to pin a project directory that lives **on a server** under a workspace in the sidebar ("like the groups logic"), and — since the SSH connection is already open — to see that folder's git worktrees so "what exists and what doesn't" is obvious at a glance. One action per worktree row: open a session there. Implemented on `claude/pane-overflow-project-folders-a7353c` (commits `568c905`, `4138d33`, `74472be`), see PROGRESS.txt 2026-08-13.
- **Decided at the same time (Yossi's calls, no separate thread needed):**
  - **The folder lives on the WORKSPACE, not as a global entity.** `Vec<ProjectFolder>` on `Workspace`, serde-defaulted and `skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty"` so an existing workspaces.json round-trips byte-identical. A global project entity with an FK back to the workspace was the alternative — rejected as more code and one more thing to keep in sync, for no gain: the folder has no meaning apart from the host its workspace points at.
  - **"Open session here" = a new pane in the current workspace**, connected with `cwdOverride`. A whole new workspace per worktree was the alternative; it isolates better but accumulates sidebar rows fast. The entire pipe (`workspace_split` → `connectPane { cwdOverride }`) already existed, so this cost zero schema.
  - **No polling.** A scan is `git worktree list --porcelain` over the SSH connection — hundreds of ms. It fires on expand and on an explicit ⟳, and the result sticks. Background refresh was offered and declined.
  - **Worktree creation is in scope, including over SSH.** `project_folder_create_worktree` runs on any transport. The pre-existing `workspace_create_worktree` is local-only AND re-anchors the whole workspace's cwd — a different job — so it was left alone rather than generalized.
- **A failed scan surfaces git's own message, never an empty list.** "No worktrees" and "you aren't connected" are different facts; collapsing them would make a disconnected workspace look like an empty repo.
- **Open risk / smoke:** nothing here has run live. Pin a folder on an SSH workspace → expand → real worktree list; click a row → new pane, verify with `pwd`; create a worktree from a branch → appears after the refresh; **disconnected** workspace → clear error, no stuck spinner; bad path → git's message. Also the byte-identical round-trip against a real workspaces.json. Until that runs this is "compiles, untested" per Rule #14.
### 2026-08-18 — Why do tmux sessions vanish server-side after an abrupt disconnect? NO MECHANISM FOUND
- **The report:** Yossi unplugged a laptop from AC and every tmux session on `runner@185.237.252.130` was gone. Crucially, closing the app cleanly does NOT do this — the sessions survive and reattach. He confirms `tmux ls` was already empty **before** the manual reboot he performed, so the reboot is not the explanation.
- **Three hypotheses falsified, in order:**
  1. *`systemd-logind` with `KillUserProcesses=yes`.* Killed by Yossi's own observation: a clean app shutdown also ends every login session, and there the tmux survives. `loginctl`/`linger` appear nowhere in the repo (only a comment at `winmux-addons/src/lib.rs:222` acknowledging the daemon's nohup fallback).
  2. *An external reboot.* The 22:21 reboot was Yossi's, done by hand because he could not connect. It is downstream of the bug.
  3. *Any kill path in this repo.* All were read and excluded: the 4 `tmux kill-session` sites are behind explicit user action; `kill_session_inner` (`lib.rs:3288`) explicitly preserves tmux; the bootstrap `pkill` never matched tmux's cmdline (`tmux -f $HOME/.winmux/tmux.conf new-session…`); the port-watch and addons pkills are scoped; the Go hygiene daemon (`server/internal/insights/hygiene.go`) permits only duplicate port-watchers by PID and never auto-kills claude. No `destroy-unattached`/`exit-empty` in the shipped conf.
- **What IS explained:** the reconnect cascade (`App.tsx`, fixed 2026-08-18) meant a full outage abandoned every pane but the last, and the SFTP storm meant the one survivor failed all five attempts. That fully accounts for "I could not reconnect, so I rebooted" — it does not account for sessions already being gone.
- **Two candidates left, both needing evidence rather than code reading:** (a) something outside winmux — the OOM killer on a shared box sitting at load average 22 with 10 users; (b) the `tmux ls` was pointed at a different socket (other user, other `TMUX_TMPDIR`) while the sessions were alive.
- **Decision: do not ship a fix on a guess.** Three confident hypotheses have already been wrong. `lib.rs` now logs a `tmux-inventory:` line before every attach so the disappearance gets a timestamp from our side; the rest is `journalctl` on the server. Tracked as FOLLOWUPS P0.

### 2026-08-12 — Tickets now write to the project ON THE AGENT'S MACHINE: LIVE SMOKE PENDING
- **What changed:** the 2026-08-11 design stored tickets app-locally for SSH workspaces and kept the remote path as metadata. Yossi: *"הרי הסוכן קוד יושב על השרת"* — correct, and it defeated the feature. A `Store` enum whose `Remote` arm carries a live SSH handle now writes into `<project>/.winmux-tickets/` on the host (`12cf043`), with the UI showing `srv-01:/path` before saving (`e0c32d6`). Local and WSL unchanged in intent, but WSL's git-root bug is fixed.
- **No second SSH connection.** The browser's port-forward and the ticket I/O are channels on the SAME russh session (`open_auto_forward`, `lib.rs:3473`, uses the same handle lookup). One TCP connection, one login. This also means "disconnected" is nearly unreachable during a capture: no SSH → no tunnel → no page to right-click.
- **Verified landmine:** `russh-sftp` 2.1.1 negotiates no posix-rename, so SFTP `rename` fails when the destination exists. Remote atomic write is `.tmp` + `mv -f` over exec. **A pure-SFTP implementation would pass every create test and fail every update.**
- **Open risks, in order:**
  1. `on_navigation` with an unregistered scheme in WebView2 — unchanged from 2026-08-11, still the thing that gates everything. Fallback: `on_web_resource_request`.
  2. `tickets_update` over SSH — the `mv -f`-over-existing case. **Smoke this specifically**; testing only create hides the bug.
  3. SVG-foreignObject screenshot fidelity — only a real page will show whether the inlined computed styles are enough.
  4. WSL `\\wsl.localhost` translation has never run against a real distro.
- **Smoke:** SSH capture → save → `ls <project>/.winmux-tickets` on the host; resolve a ticket (update); list + delete; drop the session after the page loads → expect clipboard, no file; WSL workspace whose `cwd` is a **subdir** → ticket must land in the repo ROOT; `git` absent on the remote → falls to the `cwd` rung; Local unchanged. Until that runs this is "compiles, untested" per CLAUDE.md.

### Decided alongside
- **Screenshots use an in-page SVG foreignObject**, not html2canvas (would inject ~200KB of third-party code into the user's page per capture, contradicting the zero-injection rule this feature has followed) and not an OS-level BitBlt from Rust (pixel-perfect but Windows-only, new PNG dep, ~150 lines of unsafe, breaks when the window is occluded). Fidelity is the accepted trade; it degrades to no image rather than to a wrong ticket.
- **Nothing is ever written to a silent fallback.** No writable destination ⇒ no Save button, and the ticket markdown goes to the clipboard so the typed description survives. This replaced a planned local-fallback-file design and removed two fields from the schema (`allow_local_fallback`, `stored_local_fallback`).
- **The project override stays a text field**, not a folder browser. Phase 54 was reverted for making the user browse an SFTP tree for something the app can derive; the override exists only for when the derivation is wrong.
- **`workspace_fs` consolidation deliberately NOT done** — logged in BACKLOG.md. Six hand-rolled SSH exec loops and four handle pickers exist; unifying them would touch a lot of working code, against Yossi's "don't touch the existing browser/app" constraint. Two real bugs it would fix are logged in FOLLOWUPS (diff_pane silently broken on remote workspaces; `addons::is_local_workspace` misclassifying WSL).

### 2026-08-11 — Browser Dev Mode + Tickets (v2): LIVE SMOKE PENDING
- **Context:** the feature existed already — 13 commits ending at `aefbba4` (Phase 54) — but **no branch contained them**; they were unreachable objects one `git gc` from gone. Rescued to `browser-dev-mode-tickets` (archive only). Yossi's call: do not restore Phase 54, redo tickets as a clean add-on that does not touch the existing browser. Rebuilt on `browser-tickets-v2` (5 commits, `3025f5e`..`7f7445b`). See PROGRESS.txt 2026-08-11.
- **Why Phase 54 never worked:** its inspect script wrote the capture to `localStorage.__winmux_ticket__` in the child webview and nothing ever read it — one occurrence of that key in the tree, the write. No IPC existed to read it back.
- **~~The one open risk~~ — CLOSED 2026-08-13.** WebView2 **does** fire `on_navigation` for the unregistered `winmux-ticket:` scheme. Live smoke on Windows: right-click opened the capture modal with the element filled in. The `on_web_resource_request` fallback is not needed.
- **Still open, narrower:** no ticket has yet been *written* to a remote server. The command shapes are proven against the live host by hand (`mkdir -p`, `mv -f` over an existing file, the bulk base64 listing), and WSL is proven end-to-end by a live test, but the russh-sftp write / `tickets_update` / remote `tickets_list` path has not run. That is the remaining smoke.
- **Smoke:** Browser → pick a port → Go → 🐞 Dev Mode → right-click an element → modal → save → Tickets panel lists it. Until that runs, this is "compiles, untested" per CLAUDE.md.

### Decided at the same time (no separate thread needed)
- **Tickets belong to a project, but the project is DERIVED and the field lives on the TICKET.** First pass dropped the project association entirely to avoid a migration — wrong, and corrected in `c66703c`: an unattached ticket cannot be handed to Claude Code in the right repo. Phase 54's actual mistake was putting `project_path` on the *Workspace* struct (workspaces.json migration + 4 initializers + provisioning + rpc_server + an SFTP folder picker for information the app already had). On the ticket instead, both goals hold with no schema movement. Resolution ladder: override (localStorage) → `git_worktree` → git root walked **up** from `cwd` → `cwd` → app-local. Storage: `<project>/.winmux-tickets/` for Local and for WSL via `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…`. **Superseded 2026-08-12 for SSH** — that entry above: SSH no longer falls back to app-local, it writes to the project on the host over the session the browser tunnel already uses. The destination is shown in the modal before every save; `.gitignore` is never touched. `dev_mode` stays in localStorage (per-machine preference, no schema).
- **Phase 54's browser tabs are dead.** `main` shipped its own (`0d4e9a4`) two hours after `aefbba4` on the same day. Main's version wins; the archive's is not being ported.
- **Capture is right-click**, not the archive's hover + left-click, which made the page unusable while Dev Mode was armed.

Only remainder: waiting-ring animation under a real blocking Claude permission (harness-verified only), and merging the worktree branch back into `redesign/winmux-directions`.

### 2026-08-19 — THE TERMINAL GRID WAS INHERITING THE UI'S DIRECTION. Root cause, found while diagnosing the local/remote split.
- **What was wrong:** `app/src/i18n/index.ts:75` sets `dir` on `<html>` from the interface language, so a Hebrew UI makes the whole document RTL. xterm.js pins no direction of its own — verified, nothing in `@xterm/xterm/css/xterm.css` and no `setAttribute("dir")` in its lib. And `.terminal-container` carried no direction rule. `dir` inherits. **So the terminal's character grid was flipped by the UI language, in every rtl_mode except `auto_per_line`** — which was the only one that explicitly set `dir="ltr"` on `.xterm-rows` (`terminalInstance.ts:971`).
- **Two symptoms it explains, both of which had resisted explanation:**
  1. Yossi reported `off` and `auto_per_line` looking identical on a freshly-opened local pane. They should differ sharply — WebGL with no bidi at all vs DOM with per-row `dir`. They looked alike because both ended up RTL, from different sources: one from the per-row pass, the other by inheriting the Hebrew UI.
  2. "מיושר לשמאל במקום לימין" on SSH panes. Alignment was never a terminal setting at all; it was the inherited paragraph direction of the grid.
- **Fix:** `direction: ltr` on `.terminal-container` (`App.css`), unconditional. A terminal is a fixed character grid — column 0 is always leftmost. Only a ROW's paragraph direction may vary, and that is set with a `dir` attribute on the row, which is a declaration on the element itself and so still beats the inherited value. `auto_per_line` is unaffected.
- **Why this was invisible for so long:** every RTL investigation in this log tested `auto_per_line`, the one mode that accidentally immunised itself by pinning the rows host to LTR. The bug only shows in the other two modes, and only for users running a Hebrew or Arabic interface — which is a small set that happens to contain exactly the person who would notice.
- **Consequence for the per-connection-kind split:** unknown until re-tested. The split may still be wanted (SSH panes run Linux Claude inside tmux; local panes run Windows Claude with no tmux), but part of what looked like a local-vs-remote difference was this leak, which was never connection-specific. **Re-measure both sides before building profiles.**
- **State:** Decided and implemented; live smoke pending.

### 2026-08-19 — TUI-owns-bidi is OBSOLETE: Claude no longer emits visual-order Hebrew. Now off by default.
- **Measured, not reasoned.** With the restored feature live (v0.4.5, `676b46b`): a LOCAL Windows pane running `claude-fable-5` scrambled Hebrew, while SSH panes were fine and the shell was fine. `debug.log` showed exactly ONE pane with transitions — `tui-owns-bidi on` then `off`, clean, no latch. So the feature engaged precisely where the breakage was, and never engaged where things worked.
- **Why SSH was fine: it never engaged there.** tmux swallows the OSC 0/2 title (no `set-titles` in the bundled conf), so the detector never fires on SSH/WSL panes. The 2026-07-17 follow-up #3 asked "why DID it engage on an SSH pane" — the question inverts: the problem is that it engages on LOCAL panes, which have no tmux in the way.
- **The premise expired.** The feature was written in 2026-07 and verified against Claude 2.1.74–2.1.210, which printed Hebrew ALREADY reversed for terminals with no BiDi support. Current Claude prints Hebrew in ordinary logical order — same as the shell, which is why the plain per-line RTL pass renders it correctly. Forcing `dir="ltr"` over logical Hebrew is now the thing that scrambles it. **Restoring this feature made local panes worse, not better.**
- **Decision: gate it, do not delete it.** New setting `terminal.tui_owns_bidi`, **default false**, with a Settings → Terminal toggle and en/he/ar/ru strings. Deleting it a third time is exactly the loop that produced this session — it was genuinely necessary one month ago and may still be for an older Claude or another self-bidi TUI. Detection and the `tui-owns-bidi` log line run **regardless of the flag**; only the LTR forcing is gated (`bidiOwnedByTui` in `terminalInstance.ts`). That instrumentation is what turned this diagnosis from days into minutes, so it stays on.
- **Process note, worth more than the fix.** The 2026-08-18 entry below records me confirming the symptom via a multiple-choice question whose first option was the answer I expected. That is a leading question, and it sent the session down a restore path when the honest first move was to ask what breaks WHERE. The `local vs SSH` split — one sentence from Yossi — collapsed the whole thing immediately. Ask for the discriminating observation before the confirming one.
- **Still open:** whether `dir="ltr"` would even have been sufficient for genuinely visual-order output. It would NOT — `dir` sets only the paragraph base direction, and UAX #9 rule L2 still reverses Hebrew runs inside an LTR paragraph; suppressing reordering needs `unicode-bidi: bidi-override`, which appears nowhere near `.xterm-rows`. So the legacy path is probably ALSO broken for the old Claude it was written for. Not fixed, not verified, and no longer urgent now that the default is off — but anyone turning the flag on should expect it not to work and should add `bidi-override` first.
- **State:** Decided and implemented; live smoke of the OFF default pending on Yossi's local pane.

### 2026-08-19 — Zellij spike, automatable half: PASSES. The decisive half needs a real terminal.
- **Yossi: "בא נעבור ונוסיף את זה במקום TMUX".** Scope question (local panes only vs replacing tmux on the Linux servers too) was asked and not answered, so this proceeded with B0 — the spike — which is step one either way and forecloses neither.
- **The Windows binary is real and official.** `winget install Zellij.Zellij` → 0.44.3, installs to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Zellij\zellij.exe` (48 MB) and adds itself to the user PATH; `zellij --version` answers. The upstream v0.44.3 release genuinely ships `zellij-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc-installer.msi` and `…-msvc.zip`. **The third-party `arndawg.zellij-windows` fork (0.43.1-win32.5) in winget is NOT needed** — no supply-chain dependency on a fork.
- **Correction to the 2026-08-18 entry below:** the heise report of "0.44.0, native Windows, 2026-03-23" does not survive contact. The installed 0.44.3 binary is dated 13 May and upstream's release notes for it are ordinary patch notes. The Windows support is real; the date and version story in that article is not. Do not plan around it.
- **The CLI surface winmux needs exists, and one piece beats tmux:**
  | need | tmux | zellij |
  |---|---|---|
  | attach-or-create | `new-session -A -s` | `attach -c NAME` |
  | create DETACHED, no client | — | `attach -b NAME` |
  | list | `ls` | `list-sessions` |
  | kill | `kill-session` | `kill-session` |
  | drop serialized state | — | `delete-session` |
  <!-- 2026-08-20: ymux sends NEITHER of those two rows separately any more —
       it sends `delete-session -f`, which does both in one verb. See the
       Decided entry for that date, and docs/ZELLIJ.md §3. -->
  `attach -b` has no tmux equivalent and is exactly what `pane_probe_tmux_sessions` wants.
- **What could NOT be tested from a tool session, and why it matters.** `zellij attach -c` / `-s` start, emit the alt-screen sequences and immediately print `Bye from Zellij!` because there is no interactive TTY; `attach -b` left no session behind for the same reason (the shell inside it exits at once). So **detach → reattach → type**, which is the whole feature and the exact thing issue #5365 reports broken on Windows, cannot be exercised here. It is ~2 minutes at a real terminal.
- **Nothing was left behind:** no sessions, no `zellij.exe` processes. It did create `%APPDATA%\zellij` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\zellij`.
- **State:** Open — blocked on the manual detach/reattach test and on the scope answer. Still zero winmux code.

### 2026-08-18 — Zellij as the Windows multiplexer: drop WSL for local panes? SPIKE GATE FIRST
- **Context:** Yossi: "להוריד את ה-WSL ולהשתמש ב-Zellij". My first assumption — that Zellij is Linux-only and this just adds a layer — was **wrong**: Zellij 0.44.0 (2026-03-23) added native Windows support via a community contribution, no WSL involved.
- **The goal is narrower and better-founded than it first sounds.** winmux is already tmux-native everywhere it matters, and `LOCAL_CAPS.tmuxPersistence = false` (`app/src/types.ts:352`) is the ONE gap — local Windows panes are the only place with no persistence, which is precisely why WSL workspaces were built (Phase 80). Everything else WSL provides already exists natively: `local_setup.rs:1220` installs Claude Code for Windows (`claude.ai/install.ps1`), and `winmux-cli.exe` is already built and staged. **Persistence is the only thing left.** If Zellij supplies it, the 8-step WSL ladder (`InstallWsl`, `CreateWslUser`, `EnableLinuxEnv`, `InstallTmuxInWsl`, `DeployWinmuxCliToWsl`, `DeployTmuxConfToWsl`, `InstallClaudeCodeInWsl`, `InstallHooksInWsl`) becomes optional rather than required.
- **Also a genuine capability gain, not just parity:** Zellij serialises sessions to disk (layout, commands, optionally viewport + scrollback). That **survives a reboot** — tmux does not, and this log already states WSL persistence dies on `wsl --shutdown`/reboot.
- **Why it is NOT being built yet.** The Windows port is 5 months old and the open issues hit exactly what we would be buying: **#5365 keyboard input dies in a pane after a detach/reattach cycle** (the single feature being purchased), **#5348 "Zellij does not run properly on Windows shells"**, **#5451 a pane renders never and ignores input forever in a multi-tab startup layout** (winmux opens panes in layouts), #5360 Ctrl+Alt bindings dead. Upstream release notes claim Windows feature-parity but document no ConPTY/shell caveats at all.
- **Decision: a manual spike gates all winmux code.** Outside the repo, on a plain terminal: attach-or-create by name → detach → reattach → **type**; 3× plus once across a full app close; a multi-pane layout; the CLI equivalents of `tmux new-session -A -s` / `tmux ls` / `tmux kill-session`; and whether resurrection really survives a Windows reboot. If detach/reattach or layout rendering fails, this is dead until upstream moves and no winmux-side design compensates. Record the result here either way.
- **If the spike passes, the shape is deliberately small:** no `Connection::Zellij` variant (that forces a `workspaces.json` migration and touches every match site for nothing) — `Connection::Local` already exists and Zellij is a wrapper over the same ConPTY spawn. Turn `LOCAL_CAPS.tmuxPersistence` into a runtime probe and generalise the field from "tmux" to "multiplexer"; `PaneView.tsx:490-496` already gates on the capability rather than "is it SSH", so the session picker, `[Connect]` probe and Kill-session button light up with no UI work. Add a sibling of `build_tmux_attach_script` rather than branching inside it (Phase 65 proved editing that function carelessly kills sessions).
- **WSL removal is a separate change with its own migration.** `Connection::Wsl` workspaces exist in users' `workspaces.json` and older builds already cannot read them.
- **State:** Open — blocked on the spike. Zero winmux code written.

### 2026-07-16 — TUI-owns-bidi (ad40fbf): reverted, restored, SILENTLY LOST IN A MERGE, re-restored 2026-08-18
- **Context:** the branch `rtl-claude-visual-order` merged into `claude/integration` WITHOUT its required live smoke (its own commit said "compiles-untested … needs live smoke per docs/RTL-TEST.md before merge"). Yossi's live test caught it: mouse selection on mixed Hebrew/English lines started from the wrong segment and the RTL caret sat one cell forward again — forcing `dir=ltr` on all rows while Claude is foreground disarms the RTL mouse-coordinate mirror (`a20f375`) and `isCurrentLineRtl()`, but the browser still bidi-reorders Hebrew runs inside LTR rows, so click→column mapping breaks.
- **Action taken (2026-07-16):** clean `git revert ad40fbf` on `claude/integration` (`1626ce4`); the `rtl-mouse:` per-click debug logging stays in.
- **RESTORED (2026-07-17):** the revert turned out far more expensive than the quirk it traded away — modern Claude emits visual-order Hebrew + bidi isolates, so WITHOUT tui-owns-bidi every Hebrew Claude screen double-bidis into garbage (see the RTL mouse/selection saga entry, now under Archived, for the A/B proof). Yossi's call: display correctness wins; the caret gap + mixed-line selection imprecision on Claude panes are the accepted cost until the follow-up round.
- **The follow-up round must include:** (1) caret alignment under tuiOwnsBidi (the gap Yossi sees when typing Hebrew to Claude); (2) mouse mapping that stays correct on forced-LTR rows with RTL runs; (3) title forwarding through tmux for remote panes (bundled conf has no `set-titles` — yet tui-owns-bidi engaged on the boost SSH pane, so verify how the title actually arrives before assuming this is needed); (4) a real live smoke per docs/RTL-TEST.md.
- **LOST IN MERGE `bcaa330` (2026-07-31), found 2026-08-18.** The line above ("feature IN") was false for 18 days. Yossi asked about RTL again — reversed/scrambled Hebrew, the exact documented symptom — and the cause was not a missing feature but a merge resolution that took the wrong side. Verified three ways: parent `456f0ed` ("round-10 diagnosis — TUI-owns-bidi restored, saga closed") has 9 `tuiOwnsBidi` occurrences in `terminalInstance.ts`, parent `45b6592` has 0, the merge result has 0, and so did `main` until today. The merge also dropped `nextTuiOwnsBidi` from `textDirection.ts` (27 lines) and its 10 unit tests.
- **Collateral from the SAME merge:** the Phase 79 `createLogger` conversion in `terminalInstance.ts` (24 logger sites → 0, `console.*` 0 → 17). `FOLLOWUPS.md` logged this on 2026-08-11 as "untouched pre-existing debt" — wrong diagnosis; it is a regression from 2026-07-31, and a Rule #9 violation. Entry corrected.
- **Re-restored 2026-08-18** by hand-applying 7 hunks (NOT `git checkout 456f0ed --`, which would have destroyed `readClipboardText()` and the WebView2 paste fix that landed after). `log.info` → `termLog.info`, the only substantive change. `textDirection.ts` is now byte-identical to `456f0ed`. 47/47 unit tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean.
- **Lesson:** a feature can die in a merge with no test failure and no compile error, and the doc that says it shipped is not evidence. `textDirection.test.ts` covered `nextTuiOwnsBidi` — but the merge deleted the tests alongside the code, so the suite stayed green. Grep the tree, not the log.
- **State:** Open — code restored and type-checks, but **LIVE SMOKE PENDING** (per CLAUDE.md, "verified" means a real run). Follow-ups (1) caret alignment, (2) mouse mapping on forced-LTR rows, (4) live smoke per `docs/RTL-TEST.md` remain open. Follow-up (3) title-forwarding through tmux is now instrumented rather than assumed — see below.
- **On follow-up (3), deliberately NOT acted on yet:** `resources/winmux-tmux.conf` has no `set-titles` (zero hits repo-wide) and tmux defaults it off, so `onTitleChange` should never fire inside tmux — yet this entry records it engaging on a live SSH pane. Rather than design around an unexplained observation, the restored `termLog.info("tui-owns-bidi …")` line now puts the answer in `debug.log`. Read it before touching the conf. **Trap if you do:** tmux's default `set-titles-string` always contains the session name, so it is never empty, `nextTuiOwnsBidi` falls through to `return prev`, and the feature latches ON permanently — worse than the bug. It must be the pair `set -g set-titles on` + `set -g set-titles-string "#T"`, placed in `build_tmux_attach_script` (lib.rs) not the conf, since the conf is skipped when `use_winmux_tmux_config` is off.
- **New interaction never tested against this feature:** Phase 81 session restore (`sessionRestore.ts`, newer than `456f0ed`) reattaches panes on boot with `tuiOwnsBidi = false` against an already-running Claude that will not re-emit its title. Part of the pending smoke.

### 2026-07-15 — Phase 79 follow-ups: smoke, rebake, build-warning cleanup
Deferred items out of the unified-logging overhaul (Phase 79) — each is a self-contained session:
- **Manual smoke with a live host (required before release):** Settings → Logs → switch to Debug → `[DEBUG]` lines appear immediately and `~/.winmux/log-level` updates on a connected host (server converges ≤30s, no restart); frontend error → `[UI:*]` line in tail + `winmux dev console-tail`; wait ~60s with a connected workspace → `[SYNC]` markers + `[SRV:*]`/`[HOOK]` lines merged into local debug.log; truncate a remote log → next cycle resumes near EOF without duplication; Clear button works.
- **Rebake embedded remote binaries — ✅ DONE (2026-07-15, same day):** Yossi flagged the deferral as wrong (the rebake was in the approved plan). `winmux-server-linux-{x64,arm64}` rebuilt with the slog migration, versions bumped 2.1.3 → 2.1.4 (`core.Version` + `INSIGHTS_VERSION`) so existing installs get the update offer (79.F); the bundled Linux CLI + manifest SHA rebaked via the tauri build (79.G). Existing remotes upgrade via Add-ons → Update.
- **ts-rs serde-attr warning noise (~73 per build):** ts-rs can't parse multi-key attrs like `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` and ignores them. Mostly noise, but there's a real gap underneath: ignored `skip_serializing_if` means generated TS types declare fields required that JSON may omit (masked today by the hand-mirrored types in `app/src/settings.ts`). Fix = ts-rs upgrade or splitting attrs across lines on ~dozens of structs. Effort: M, mechanical.
- **Vite chunk-size warning** — 1.3 MB minified bundle > 500 kB limit. Real fix = code-splitting/dynamic imports; low priority for a local desktop webview (no network fetch). Effort: M-L. Snooze candidate: raise `chunkSizeWarningLimit` to silence.
- **State:** Open — smoke belongs to the next release cut (rebake ✅ done); `renumber_group_list` ✅ deleted 2026-08-03 (`251b3c3`… dead helper, group-reorder renumbers inline). Remaining: ts-rs serde-attr + Vite chunk-size, two independent cleanup sessions.

     a CLI agent multiplexer, v0.5.9 / 906★ as of May 2026. Ideas, not commitments. -->

### 2026-07-13 — Notification delivery matrix (per-hook target)
- **Idea:** Configurable notification target — user chooses, per hook, where a notification lands: system toast / terminal notification / in-app Feed only.
- **Reference:** [herdr v0.5.1](https://github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr) — `ui.toast.delivery = "system"|"terminal"|"in-app"` (v0.5.1 changelog).
- **Rationale:** winmux currently emits only a Windows system toast. A delivery matrix gives users control — e.g. silent/low-priority hooks route to the Feed only, high-priority to system toast + sound.
- **Effort:** M
- **State:** Open — deferred to post-beta.3 hooks work.

### 2026-07-13 — Hook stale / out-of-order state audit
- **Idea:** Audit hook-event processing for stale, out-of-order reports; base notifications on the *effective* agent state rather than raw hook arrival order.
- **Reference:** [herdr v0.5.8](https://github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr) — "Hook-based agent integrations now reject stale out-of-order reports and base notifications on effective agent state, reducing duplicate or stuck state changes" (v0.5.8 changelog).
- **Rationale:** winmux has deep hook wiring (RPC bridge → Feed). Audit whether stale/duplicate hooks produce duplicate Feed cards or missed state transitions.
- **Effort:** S+M (audit S, fix M).
- **State:** Open — deferred to post-beta.3.

### 2026-07-13 — Live config reload (no restart)
- **Idea:** Live-reload settings from the Settings modal or CLI (`winmux server reload-config`) without an app restart.
- **Reference:** [herdr v0.5.2](https://github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr) — "Config can now be reloaded in the running app/server from the global menu or with `herdr server reload-config`" (v0.5.2 changelog).
- **Rationale:** Some winmux settings currently require a restart to take effect. Live reload improves DX. Needs enumerating which settings are safely live-reloadable.
- **Effort:** M
- **State:** Open — deferred to v0.5.x.
