## Decided

### 2026-09-02 — Phase 90: the active-sessions overview, and tmux rename comes back (ASCII-only)
- **Context:** Yossi wanted, from a workspace's right-click menu (above Add-ons), a full-screen table of every tmux/zellij session on that machine with an agent-written one-line summary and status per row, grouped by project folder, with Open / Kill / Rename per row.
- **Decided (Yossi, asked before building):** scope is SSH + macOS tmux **and Windows zellij** (list + summaries via `dump-screen`; grouping falls back to the claim-time `owner_cwd` since zellij reports no cwd). The table renders first and summaries fill in asynchronously. Status is the model's read of the screen (`idle | working | waiting_input | error`), attached/detached stays a tmux badge.
- **Decided — Rename is a real `tmux rename-session`, ASCII-only.** This lifts the 2026-07-16 line "NO tmux rename anywhere (Phase 23.J crash constraint stands)" for an explicit user action inside `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`. The 23.I crash was a Hebrew name and was never root-caused, so non-ASCII keeps going through labels; `pane_set_title` still never renames. The command migrates owners, labels, live `Session.tmux_session` fields and the SSH session-meta label; `auto_name` / `claude_title` for the old key are lost until the next Claude turn. **zellij rename stays refused** (`docs/ZELLIJ.md` §1 — the name is how a cold start finds the session).
- **Decided — one model call per chunk, on the machine that holds the sessions.** Over SSH the capture loop and `claude -p` are a single remote pipeline, so screen bytes never reach the desktop; locally they are piped into the local `claude` on stdin. Sessions are indexed, not named, in the prompt. Legacy WSL workspaces list but get no summaries — nothing creates new ones.
- **Decided — no new list command.** `pane_list_tmux_sessions` with `project_path: null` plus one new field (`owner_cwd`) is the list; `KillTarget` / `kill_target` were lifted out of the pane-bound kill rather than duplicated.
- **Status:** compiles-untested until CI; Rule #14 until the FOLLOWUPS P1 smoke list runs live.

#### Addendum, 2026-09-02 (90.B) — "פתח" opens the session on a screen of its own
- **Context.** The first cut's Open split/tabbed the CURRENT workspace. Yossi: *"בפתח - אל תפצל את המסך לעוד - תוסיף שורה תחת המכונה (בעץ) והסשן יפתח על מסך משל עצמו. אם ידועה תיקיית העבודה תפתח תחת האיבר בעץ של תיקיית העבודה."*
- **Decided (Yossi, asked before building):** the row is a **real persisted child workspace** (same mechanism as a pinned folder / worktree; delete = detach only, the session lives). Placement: under the pinned project folder whose `cwd` equals/contains the session cwd, deepest wins; **no such folder → directly under the server, never auto-pin**. A second Open on the same session **activates the existing row** (idempotent on the new `Workspace.tmux_session`). Row name = the session's display name.
- **Found on the way, required:** activation never auto-connects panes, so a persisted row would show [Connect] after a restart and a click would spawn a NEW pane-derived session. `connectPane` now defaults to `ws.tmux_session` for the workspace's first pane, and `restoreSessions` uses it when localStorage has no hint (second machine). Without those the row is a lie after a restart.
- **Status:** compiles-untested until CI; smoke list in FOLLOWUPS P1.

#### Addendum, 2026-09-02 (90.C) — summaries are pulled, not pushed; and the phase is 90, not 87
- **Context.** Yossi, relayed through the BRIEF session onto PR #41: *"צריך לאפשר בחירה, על איזה סשנים אני רוצה לראות סיכום ומה לא."* Every chunk is a real `claude -p` over captured screens; a machine holds many sessions he does not care about.
- **Decided:** default = NO auto-summarize. Per-row tick (and a per-folder tick on the group heading) + one **Summarize selected (N)** action that batches the ticked rows into the existing single-pipeline call. Selection is per open (v1); per-host persistence (the `session-owners.json` keying precedent) stays in BACKLOG. Showing a fresh self-reported BRIEF for free instead of an LLM call is the BACKLOG unification item, deferred.
- **Renumbered:** this work was labelled Phase 87 on the branch, but 87 is the recordable-shortcuts phase (on main since 2026-08-23) and 89 is the highest in use — it is **Phase 90** (90.A the capture-pane target fix, 90.B Open-as-workspace, 90.C selection). The three PR #41 commit messages still say 87; history is not rewritten.

### 2026-09-01 — BRIEF: agent-written briefs, Queue panel, Briefing card (context switching level 2)
- **Context:** Yossi's CEO-brief spec for managing parallel sessions ("the session writes the brief; you only read and decide"), plus a live reference table he generated by hand-running Claude over his server's sessions (grouped by project, status 🔄/⏸️/💤/✅, a rich "what's happening" column). Full plan: `.claude/plans/precious-finding-dragon.md`; discussion in this session's PROGRESS entries.
- **Decided — the agent writes the brief, the desktop only parses.** A `[ymux-brief]` block (task/status/ask/rec/next/delta) at the end of the final assistant message. Chosen over (a) CLI reading a BRIEF.md from cwd (new IO + CLI re-converge everywhere) and (b) LLM summarization per stop (tokens + latency per turn) because the Stop payload's `last_assistant_message` already reaches the desktop verbatim — zero CLI changes, stale remote CLIs keep working.
- **Decided — decision-first, not history-first.** The card/queue lead with `ask · rec` (a closed question + the agent's recommendation); history stays in git. No marker → degraded brief (status+delta only, never a fabricated ask).
- **Decided — live hook state outranks the brief for placement.** `PaneAgentState` stays the only source of the traffic light; `BriefStatus` refines the ended bucket (stuck/waiting-for-you/done). Queue is grouped by workspace with counts (the reference's shape), statuses 🔄 working / ⏸️ waiting-for-you (⚠️ stuck) / 💤 done / ✅ session-ended.
- **Decided — the queue's running rows show the user's last prompt** ("got from you: …", clipped, in-memory, UI-only — Rule #1 keeps it out of every log).
- **Decided — everything opt-in.** Entry-card triggers (workspace-return via `last_active_at`, idle-return via new frontend input tracking) each have their own toggle; the manual shortcut always works. Intent is a passive per-workspace field on `Workspace` (schema v3).
- **Decided — adoption via a copy-paste CLAUDE.md snippet** in the Queue's empty state, not a seeded skill (the skills registry ships empty and seeding is new plumbing). Deferred to BACKLOG: `ymux-brief` skill seeding, LLM "refresh the picture" button over transcripts.

### 2026-08-23 — Phase 86: server + port-watch + hooks load
- **Context:** measured on Yossi's server — 18 `ymux port-watch` at ~4.7% CPU each, 15 orphans (ppid=1); hook-debug.log 100% stale-reconnect noise from them; ymux-server sampling 3.1s every 5s because Docker one-shot stats block 1-2s per container. Full numbers in PROGRESS.txt (2026-08-23 ~20:30).
- **Decided — the watcher owns its own death.** stdin-EOF watchdog + no last.env fallback + exit after 3 connect failures. Hooks KEEP the fallback (Phase 80 (f)); only the watcher's tunnel is fixed for life.
- **Decided — one watcher per host, not per workspace.** Sibling workspaces on the same (host,port,user) subscribe to the owner's events; the desktop fans `port.opened/closed` out. The remote process never learns about workspaces beyond the owner's id.
- **Decided — orphans are reaped server-side by ppid, not by any "connected" registry.** There is no remote record of live workspaces (last.env is a single global file); ppid==1 on an SSH exec child is the one reliable signal, verified on 15 real cases.
- **Deferred — netlink `sock_diag` (LISTEN-only query) instead of /proc/net/tcp.** That is the fix for the kernel half of the tick cost; needs raw netlink code. BACKLOG.
- **Deferred — a resident hook agent / connection pooling.** Each hook is its own process by Claude Code's design; one connection per hook is the floor without a daemon in the loop. BACKLOG.
- **Decided (Yossi, same day) — `manifest.json` hooks version goes to 1.6.0 in this PR, not the release checklist.** The reason it was held back (bannering installs before a CLI that honours the spec exists) no longer bites: the bootstrap's every-connect `setup-hooks` now rewrites stale entries by itself, so the banner is only the path for users who turned `hooks.auto_install` off.
### 2026-08-23 — Phase 85.C: the Browser pops out with the FULL chrome, and its X just closes

- **Context:** three Browser complaints from Yossi in one message — the DevTools button
  did nothing, X left the native webview stuck over the panels, and there was no way to
  make the Browser a real separate window. The first two were bugs (85.A / 85.B); this is
  the third. It finally lands what `63.E` described and never shipped: the 63.A schema
  (`FloatingWindowMode::PopOut`, `popout_rect`, `popout_display`) had been sitting in
  `settings.json` since 2026-06 with zero readers. The 2026-06-18 Phase 63 entry was
  closed in the 2026-07-15 cleanup as "covered by later work" — for 63.E specifically
  that was not true, which is why this is a new entry rather than a reopened one.
- **Decision 1 — full chrome, not a bare browser window.** The alternative was a
  `WebviewUrl::External(url)` window: ~70 lines, no port picker, no tabs. Yossi chose the
  full chrome, so the popout loads ymux's own `index.html`, routes on the
  `browser-popout-<ws>` label, and hosts its own child webview. Cost: `BrowserWindow.tsx`
  had to be split into an injectable chrome (`BrowserChrome.tsx`) plus two hosts.
- **Decision 2 — closing the popped-out window closes the Browser.** It does NOT
  re-attach into the floating panel, unlike the terminal pop-out (`popout:closed` →
  re-attach). Simpler, and it matches what an OS window's X means everywhere else.
- **Decision 3 — the mode persists.** Quit popped out, launch popped out, same monitor
  and size. This is what makes the 63.A fields load-bearing at last, and it forced
  `floating_windows` to become Rust-owned with a carry in `settings_save`.
- **Accepted cost — popping out RELOADS the page.** A child Webview cannot be
  re-parented; `add_child` binds it to its host window for life. So the pop-out destroys
  the child under `main` and the new window spawns its own. Not fixable without giving up
  the native-child architecture. The button tooltip says so in all four locales.
- **Note for whoever revisits 63.B/C ("Pane" mode):** the same `slotRect()` +
  `windowLabel` seam this phase introduced is what a docked Pane mode would use — it
  would be a third host, not a third code path.

### 2026-08-23 — `force_rtl`: a full-RTL terminal mode for remote panes, and why it is a fourth mode rather than a fix to the third
- **Context:** Yossi asked for "RTL מלא - ולא שורה שורה - במרוחק". The existing default, `auto_per_line`, decides a paragraph direction for **every row independently** — the `RTL_DOMINANCE` vote, block-aware table/fence grouping, and `stripPaneFrame`. Each of those heuristics was added to fix a real reported bug, and each is a compromise. The ask was to stop deciding at all on remote panes.
- **Decided — force `dir="rtl"` on every row; do NOT flip the grid.** A terminal is a fixed character grid: column 0 is always leftmost. Whole-grid RTL has already been tried here **by accident** — `i18n/index.ts` sets `dir` on `<html>` from the interface language, so a Hebrew UI flipped the terminal's grid — and it was filed as a root-cause bug, fixed with an unconditional `direction: ltr` on `.terminal-container`. `force_rtl` therefore changes only each row's paragraph direction; `rowsHost` keeps `dir="ltr"` and the grid origin is untouched. Everything downstream that computes a column from a pixel offset (selection, mouse, caret, fit) keeps working.
- **Rejected — a single whole-screen bidi pass.** Terminal rows are not a paragraph. A wrapped line, a table and a status bar share a screen with no paragraph boundaries between them, so one UAX #9 run over the viewport has no correct base to resolve against.
- **Rejected — tuning `auto_per_line` instead.** The knobs already exist (`auto_direction`, `direction_policy`) and none of them expresses "always RTL". Adding a fourth value to `direction_policy` would also have made the mode reachable on `local`, where the ConPTY buffer is visual order and forcing RTL reverses letters.
- **Decided — remote only, and opt-in.** The mode ships in the radio list for both profiles, but `RTL_FIELD_DEFAULTS` and `default_rtl_mode` are unchanged: both profiles still default to `auto_per_line`. This preserves the standing invariant that local and remote are totally separated ("תוודא שיש הפרדה מוחלטת בין המרוחק למקומי"), so nothing here can reach a local pane unless a user picks it.
- **Accepted cost, stated up front:** a POSITIONAL row — tmux's status line, a zellij frame, `vim`, `htop`, Claude Code — is full-width, so UAX #9 rule L2 reverses the order of its runs and the layout renders mirrored. `RTL_DOMINANCE` and `stripPaneFrame` exist to prevent exactly that; `force_rtl` trades it away deliberately in exchange for a shell that is unconditionally right-to-left. Reverting is one click and takes effect live.
- **Two traps found while building it,** both of which reverse letters and look identical in a screenshot: (1) `normaliseIncomingToLogical` must stay pinned to `auto_per_line` — it asks whether the incoming BUFFER is visual order (a local/ConPTY condition), not whether the mode paints a `dir`; (2) `unicode-bidi: bidi-override` had to be gated on the mode rather than on `suppress` alone, since the override paints bytes verbatim and is only ever right while the buffer is still visual.
- **Also fixed in passing:** `installRtlMouseCapture` ran once in `finishConstruction`, so a pane built on WebGL (`off` / `bidi_reorder`) and switched **live** to a row-dir mode got `dir="rtl"` rows with no coordinate mirroring — clicks and drag-selection landed on the mirrored column until the pane was reopened. It is now idempotent and re-run from `applyRenderer`.
- **Status:** implemented, unit-tested (`rowDirections` in `textDirection.test.ts`, 75 cases green), **not yet verified live** — Rule #14.

### 2026-08-23 — Phase 84: panes as tabs, and Notification comes back as pane state (not as a feed item)
- **Context:** Users asked for two things that turned out to be mostly built already. "Open panes as tabs instead of splitting" is Phase 55-A's maximize plus a control surface — that feature has been swapping the split tree for a single leaf since it shipped, with the other panes' PTYs alive in the `terms` registry. "A traffic light on the tab" is a new rendering of the per-pane turn state machine that has run end-to-end since issue #4, currently painted as the `⏱ 3:10 · avg 40s` ticker.
- **Decided — tabs are a `bool` on `Workspace`, not a `LayoutNode::Tabs` variant.** The variant was the obvious model and is the wrong one: it makes every `match` on `LayoutNode` non-exhaustive (~44 sites in the app crate alone), it is lossy in both directions — a tree collapsed into a tab list cannot get its ratios back — and an older ymux reading a `Tabs` node out of `workspaces.json` cannot render it, which is exactly what the 3-way reconcile in `workspaces_merge.rs` exists to survive. With the flag, tabs are just `collect_panes(layout)` in DFS order and the tree is never touched.
- **Decided — per-workspace, not global (Yossi, 2026-08-23).** One workspace can be a tab stack while another stays a split grid.
- **Decided — the light renders on the pane header too, not only on tabs (Yossi).** ~15 lines more than tabs-only, and it means the feature works in split mode, which is still the default.
- **Decided — `Notification` is registered again, reversing half of the v1.2.0 decision.** v0.4.4 dropped it as observability-only noise. That judgement was right *about feed items*: a card per event that nobody acted on. It is also the only signal Claude Code emits for "the agent is blocked on the human" (`permission_prompt`, `idle_prompt`, `agent_needs_input`, the elicitation dialogs), which is the entire red light. It returns with a different job — the CLI filters on `notification_type` and pushes the type alone, the desktop folds it into per-pane state and **returns before any `FeedItem` is built**. That early return is load-bearing; without it the old noise comes straight back.
- **Decided — no PTY-output scraping fallback, now or later in this shape.** `osc_notify.rs` works because OSC 9/99/777 is a documented, stable protocol. There is no documented stdout marker for Claude Code's turn state; detecting it means pattern-matching TUI chrome — spinner glyphs, the "esc to interrupt" line, permission-box borders — which is undocumented, locale-dependent (we ship he/ar/ru), theme-dependent, and changes across releases. The failure mode is precisely the one the feature exists to eliminate: the heuristic quietly stops matching after an upstream release, the light **sticks on green**, and nobody notices because there is no error. **A wrong light is worse than no light.** A hookless pane gets no light plus the existing outdated-hooks banner. If a fallback is ever wanted it should be *cooperative* (something Claude Code itself invokes), not scraped.
- **Decided — yellow does not decay on a timer.** "Claude finished and you have not dealt with it" is a lasting fact, and glancing at a strip of tabs to see who is done IS the use case; a `HOOK_PULSE_WINDOW_MS`-style decay would destroy it. That 4s decay is right for a *pulse* ("something just happened") and wrong for a *state*. The decay that exists is semantic and free: `idle_prompt` fires once Claude has actually been waiting, promoting Done → NeedsInput. Yellow means "just finished", red means "finished and now waiting on you". Yellow also does **not** clear on focus — the pulse clears on focus because a pulse is an unseen event; the light is the agent's current state, and conflating them produces a light that goes dark while Claude is still sitting there waiting.
- **Decided — `PermissionRequest` stays unregistered.** It overlaps entirely with `Notification/permission_prompt` and with our own PreToolUse gate, and it is a *blocking* hook — registering it puts a second synchronous round-trip on the tool-call critical path for zero new information.
- **Known lie, stated deliberately:** a turn that dies on an API error shows **yellow "your turn"**, because `StopFailure` is not registered. Logged in FOLLOWUPS; it is the largest remaining inaccuracy in v1.
- **Not decided / deferred:** bumping `manifest.json`'s `hooks.claude-code.version` to 1.5.0. It is required for anyone to be told to re-sync, but doing it in the feature commit banners every current 0.4.5 user before a CLI exists that can honour it. It belongs in the release checklist.

### 2026-08-23 — The vault: MD pages that explain the code, with a CI freshness gate
- **Context (Yossi):** agents burn tokens re-reading source to answer "what does this do / where does X live". Wanted a "כספת" of MD files explaining the code, read instead of the code — and, crucially, a check that runs **before merging from a branch or a separate worktree** so the vault is never out of date. Note this is unrelated to the Secrets Vault thread still open above; same word, different thing.
- **What was already there:** `docs/MODULES.md` was exactly this idea, written once and never checked. By 2026-08-23 it claimed `lib.rs (~1760 lines)` against a real 12,475, covered 11 of 153 frontend files, and mentioned none of the 8 crates or the Go server. So the missing half was never the writing — it was anything that notices when the writing stops being true.
- **Options considered:** (A) diff-only gate, matching the existing server-rebake step — simple, but blind to drift that predates the PR and trips on every typo fix; (B) hash-only — catches everything but can be satisfied by re-stamping the lock without touching the prose; (C) both, with an explicit escape hatch.
- **Decision (Yossi, 2026-08-23): C, full sweep in round one, enforced in `ci-windows.yml`.** `scripts/vault-check.mjs` hashes every file a page `covers:` against `docs/vault/.vault-lock.json` **and** requires the owning `.md` in the diff. `[vault-skip]` in the PR title skips the diff half with a `::notice::` — included deliberately, because unlike a server rebake a docs gate trips on every typo inside a covered file, and a gate people route around is worse than one they use.
- **Outcome:** Phase 85 / 85.A / 85.B / 85.C / 85.D. 16 pages, 213 files, 89,874 of 93,998 tracked lines (96%). What is uncovered is generated SDK output, generated ts-rs bindings, test files, and a 2-line vite shim. `docs/MODULES.md` is now a pointer stub. CLAUDE.md Rule #18.
- **Renumbered to 85 (2026-08-23).** PR #18 landed on main using Phase 84 / 84.A / 84.B for the tabs +
  traffic-light work while this was in flight. Theirs landed first, so this branch was renumbered on
  Yossi's explicit instruction — the one force-push it will ever take. Commits before `beb87da` in any
  clone fetched earlier still say Phase 84; `git log --grep "the vault"` finds this work regardless.
- **The honest limit:** the gate proves a page *changed*, not that it became *correct*. Nothing automated can prove the latter. The human check is the one in the plan's verification section — answer a real question ("where is the SSH auth chain") from `docs/vault/INDEX.md` alone, without opening a `.rs` file. If you cannot, the page is too thin.

### 2026-08-23 — Mac builds stay ad-hoc signed: no Apple Developer membership for now
- **Context:** With no Developer ID secrets on the repo every mac build takes the ad-hoc path — `codesign -dvvv` reports `Signature=adhoc` / `TeamIdentifier=not set`, and `spctl` **rejects** both bundles. The dmgs run locally; a user who *downloads* one is blocked by Gatekeeper with "ymux is damaged and can't be opened", a message that is misleading but is what they will actually see. The workflow's signed path is written and waiting on five secrets, which require a paid membership (~$99/yr).
- **Options considered:** (A) join the programme and notarise — downloads open cleanly; (B) stay ad-hoc and document the `xattr` step; (C) drop mac distribution entirely and let contributors build from source.
- **Decision (Yossi, 2026-08-23): B — not paying right now.** So the `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` step is not a temporary workaround any more, it IS the macOS install procedure, and the README was reworded to say so plainly instead of implying notarisation was imminent. Users who will not run that command are pointed at building from source, where the quarantine flag never appears.
- **What this costs, stated honestly:** the step asks a user to disable the check that would warn them about a tampered download, on an app that also asks for SSH access. That is a real ask, and it is why the README keeps the "only for builds from this project's releases page" caveat rather than presenting the command as routine.
- **Revisit when:** mac stops being a tester-only platform — a public release, an install count worth the friction, or the first support thread that turns out to be a Gatekeeper block rather than a bug. It is a spend decision, so it does not expire on its own; someone has to raise it.
- **If it is ever reversed, the warning that matters:** the developer-id path has **never executed once**. notarytool, stapling, and the `spctl rejected a Developer ID signed build` error branch all run for the first time on whichever build follows the secrets landing — budget a failed run or two, and do not schedule it against a release cut.

### 2026-08-23 — main and the 0.5.0 line are one tree again; the two multiplexers are a deliberate split
- **Context:** `v0.5.0-beta.1` was tagged from `claude/worktrees-clean-build-988849`, not from `main`, and the two then diverged architecturally — 64 commits on the branch (zellij replaces WSL, the RTL chain, the build work) against 24 on `main` (all of Phase 82, the macOS unification). Yossi asked to "align main to the released version" and to clear out every branch and worktree.
- **Decided (Yossi):** merge `main` INTO the branch, gate on CI, then fast-forward `main` — rather than resetting `main` to the tag. A reset was the literal reading of the request and would have deleted the macOS port along with the CLAUDE.md section that describes it. Doing the conflict work on the branch keeps it revertible: `archive/pre-merge-main` and `archive/pre-merge-release` were pushed before anything moved.
- **Decided — one persistence parameter, two backends, chosen by `cfg`.** The collision was not incidental: both sides had implemented *the same feature* — a persistent local pane — with a different multiplexer. `spawn_local_pty` now takes one `persist_session: Option<String>`; `cfg(windows)` types a `zellij attach -c` line, `cfg(not(windows))` types the tmux attach script, both at the same 900ms. Each platform KEPT ITS OWN DEFAULT (`persistent.unwrap_or(true)` on Windows, `false` elsewhere) — the branch made zellij the whole point of the native-local path, and the macOS port deliberately left a plain shell unless asked. Averaging those would have been a silent behaviour change on both.
- **Decided — no persistence group on Windows.** The standing FOLLOWUPS design said to keep the wizard group and give Windows `["InstallZellij"]`. Reading the code changed that: `InstallZellij` is already an ordinary `TOOL_ROWS` entry, so a group would have offered the same install twice. The group is `<Show when={mac}>`-only; Windows sees the workspace name/cwd block alone.
- **`finalize_wsl_workspace` was deleted, not resolved.** The auto-merge left two finalizers and two call arms, and main's WSL one referenced a `distro` that was no longer a parameter — it could not have compiled. WSL left the wizard on 2026-08-19 by decision; existing `Connection::Wsl` workspaces still load.
- **Append-only docs are not `--union` files.** `main` and the branch carried the same five FOLLOWUPS entries under the pre- and post-rename spelling (`~/.winmux/bin` vs `~/.ymux/bin`), and Phase 81.F was already in the branch's DECISIONS. A blind union would have re-created exactly the duplication `f98e079` was written to remove. Every doc resolution here was checked by diffing the result against BOTH parents for lines lost from either side — which is also what caught a resolver bug that truncated three i18n files to zero bytes mid-merge.
- **Branches and worktrees:** 13 branches with zero unique commits deleted (9 already in `main`, 4 already in the release line), plus `browser-dev-mode-tickets` and `design-pass-01` — local-only, never pushed, deleted on Yossi's call without archiving. Kept open: `claude/macos-universal-signing` (Developer ID signing — the answer to the standing macOS-signing FOLLOWUPS item), `claude/check-interesting-forks-ih233q`, `hook-pane-resolve`.
- **Status:** Rule #14 — green on both workflows is "compiles and passes tests". The merge rewrote the local-pane persistence path on BOTH platforms and none of it has run; the live checklist is FOLLOWUPS P1.

### 2026-08-19 — build speed: what was actually slow, and what we chose not to do
- **Context:** Yossi: "the build takes 12-15 minutes." Measured before changing anything, which turned out to matter — most of the intuitive suspects were wrong.
- **The two real causes, both verified, not inferred.**
  1. `crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]` in `app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` — the Tauri *mobile* template default, in a repo with no `gen/android` and no `gen/apple`. Desktop links the rlib only (`main.rs` does `app_lib::run()`), so every build also archived a **215 MB** `app_lib.lib` and did a second full link of the whole object graph down to a 456 KB DLL nothing loads. Now `["rlib"]`. Confirmed after the change: the release build emits `libapp_lib-<hash>.rlib` and nothing else.
  2. **GitHub scopes cache reads to the current branch plus the default branch**, and `build-windows.yml` runs only on `workflow_dispatch` and `v*` tags — never on `main`. So a build dispatched from a fresh branch compiled all ~740 lockfile packages from scratch. Same workflow, same week: run `32187049792` logged `No cache found` and spent **813s** inside "Build release bundles"; run `32193464505` restored ~696 MB and spent **420s**. `warm-rust-cache.yml` now maintains that release-profile cache on `main` under `shared-key: windows-release`.
- **Rejected: `rust-lld` for the MSVC host link.** Tried it, measured it, reverted it. Same relink (touch `main.rs`, `tauri build --no-bundle`, warm tree): **199s with link.exe, 191s with rust-lld** — 4%, inside the noise, because that window is dominated by recompiling the 9.4k-line `app_lib` at `opt-level = 3`, not by linking. Not worth an unusual linker on a shipped binary (manifest embedding, `.res` resources) for 7 seconds. Do not re-litigate without a new measurement.
- **Measured after the change, on CI, both cache states** (`build-windows` on `claude/optimize-build-process-18f637`):

  | | before | after | build step |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | cold cache | 15m37s | **16m05s** | 813s -> 798s |
  | warm cache | 9m24s | **7m06s** | 420s -> 336s |

  Warm is the number that matters, because `warm-rust-cache.yml` is what makes warm the normal case for a branch that has never built. Cold got marginally *worse* in total wall clock: the build step did drop 15s, but a cold run now also pays to *save* a ~700 MB cache it previously never wrote — which is the whole point, and a one-time cost.

  Breakdown of the warm 336s, from the `[timing]` lines the scripts now emit: musl `ymux` **29.6s**, host `ymux` **16.1s**, Vite **14s**, the app crate **232s**, NSIS **35s**. Cold, the same legs are 76.8s / 42.7s / 14s / **622s** / 27s.

- **Two estimates from planning that the measurements killed.** Worth writing down so they are not repeated: dropping the MSI was guessed at 1.5-2.5 min and is worth roughly **30-60s** (NSIS alone runs in 27-35s, so WiX was never the monster it looked like); and the `crate-type` fix cannot be isolated from this data — warm went 420s -> 336s with *both* changes in, and 84s is the honest combined figure, not 84s for either one.
- **Two things the experiment settled empirically, worth keeping:**
  - `target.<triple>.linker` **is** in Cargo's fingerprint on 1.95 — adding the section and removing it both triggered a large rebuild (6m56s and 6m47s). So there was no stale-artifact hazard in toggling it, contrary to the usual warning.
  - The last measurement build ran `npm run tauri build` **directly**, bypassing `build-release.ps1`, and `grep -c -a $USERNAME app.exe` came back **296** where a wrapper build gives **0**. That is the new `build-windows.yml` assert firing on exactly the case it was written for, demonstrated rather than assumed. It also shows the RUSTFLAGS fingerprint working: no release can silently reuse that binary, because the wrapper's flags force a rebuild.
- **Rejected: a parallel job for the musl cross-build.** `windows-latest` runners are 4 vCPU; both legs are CPU-bound, so concurrency moves the work rather than removing it, and a second job costs ~1.5 min of checkout + toolchain + cache restore plus an artifact handoff that could ship a mismatched CLI. Warm, the musl leg measured **12.6s** and the host leg **17.6s** locally — it was only ever a cold-cache cost.
- **Rejected: `lto` / lower `codegen-units`.** Both trade build time for runtime, in the wrong direction for the stated problem.
- **What the sha256 staging guard does and does not buy.** Locally it stops the app crate rebuilding on every build and stops `remote-manifest.json` churning in the working tree. It does **not** quieten `ci-windows.yml`'s drift notice — run `32199948594` still emitted `remote-manifest sha256 changed`, with `fbf2c01c` / 2,498,992 bytes against the committed `8ecb0623` / 2,521,136. That is the toolchain gap in FOLLOWUPS P2 (CI takes `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`, Yossi's box is 1.95.0), untouched here and still needing a `rust-toolchain.toml` decision.
- **Kept deliberately: `cargo:rerun-if-changed=../dist`.** It costs an app-crate rebuild on every build that runs Vite, and it is the guard against build #5's shipped-stale-JS incident. Yossi's stated worry about caching is exactly this failure mode; the fix for slowness must not reopen it.
- **The scrub protection was fiction and is now real.** `build-release.ps1` deleted `app.exe` and `deps/app-*` to "force" the `--remap-path-prefix` rebuild. The glob matched nothing (bin artifacts in `deps/` are unhashed) and `release/app.exe` is a hardlink Cargo re-creates for fresh units — so it never forced anything. The actual guarantee is RUSTFLAGS being part of Cargo's fingerprint. Deleted the block; `build-windows.yml` now *asserts* `$USERNAME` is absent from `app.exe`, the check `docs/RELEASING.md` has always described and CI never ran.
### 2026-08-20 — Zellij is a persistence layer, not a multiplexer the user drives
- **Context:** Yossi, on seeing panes still framed a day after `pane_frames false` shipped: *"each of our panes should hold exactly ONE zellij pane"*, *"let it be locked, no way to change it"*, and *"session disconnect doesn't really work today — it should be a delete command"*. All three were correct, and the first two had the same root as the third.
- **The frame was never a frame.** `pane_frames false` removes the border AROUND a pane. The two rows above and below are PLUGIN PANES in zellij's default layout (`tab-bar`, `status-bar`), captured with `zellij setup --dump-layout default`. No config key removes them; only a layout that does not ask for them. `resources/layouts/ymux.kdl` is `layout { pane }`, and every pane gets two rows back.
- **Decided — full lock (Yossi's call).** Zero keybinds (`keybinds clear-defaults=true { }`), `default_mode "locked"`, single-pane layout, `mouse_mode false`, and the startup-tips/release-notes panes off. The alternative — locked-but-escapable via Ctrl+g — was rejected: every leak returns the moment someone presses it, including a split with no frame to separate the two shells.
  - **What it buys, and it is invisible in a screenshot:** `Ctrl-o`, `Ctrl-p`, `Ctrl-n`, `Ctrl-t`, `Ctrl-h`, `Ctrl-s`, `Ctrl-g` stop being eaten before the program inside the pane sees them. `Ctrl-o` alone is bash's operate-and-get-next.
  - **What it costs, decided rather than discovered:** no scrollback inside a zellij pane. No keybinds means no scroll mode, `mouse_mode false` means zellij never sees the wheel, and xterm.js's wheel scrolls its normal buffer, which sits behind zellij's alt screen. Accepted; a ymux-side "show scrollback" affordance on `dump-screen --full` is in BACKLOG.
- **Decided — the layout is found through `ZELLIJ_CONFIG_DIR`, not a `layout_dir` path.** A bundled resource ships identical bytes to every machine and cannot know its install directory, and `zellij attach` has no `--layout` to pass one. Verified with `setup --check`: `ZELLIJ_CONFIG_DIR=<dir>` moves `[LAYOUT DIR]` to `<dir>\layouts` and leaves DATA/PLUGIN dirs alone. Resolution is all-or-nothing — config without layout would promise a `default_layout` that resolves to nothing, and zellij is not loud about that.
- **Decided — Kill means `delete-session -f`.** The old sequence was `kill-session`, falling through to `delete-session` only if the kill FAILED. On a live session the kill succeeds, so delete never ran and the session came back EXITED-and-resurrectable. The two-step was designed as a fail-safe and **could not be reached from the UI at all**: the first click drops the pane from `pane_sessions` so a second returns early with no verb sent, and the button is gated on `isConnected`/`isTmux()`, both false by then. A safety valve nobody can operate is not a safety valve. Yossi chose the single forcing verb; the cost — no resurrecting a killed session — is the point.
- **Decided — session restore does NOT filter EXITED, deliberately.** The obvious "fix" for a killed session coming back is `.filter(s => !s.exited)` at App.tsx. It would delete the feature: a Windows reboot leaves EVERY zellij session EXITED, and resurrecting them is the one thing zellij does that tmux cannot — the reason it was adopted. The anti-resurrection guarantee belongs in the kill, and now lives there.
- **Decided — closing a pane does not kill its session.** Symmetric with SSH and WSL, where the X is a detach. The session survives and is re-attachable from the Connect picker; a `log_info` now names it so "where did my session go" has a trail. Keeping the restore HINT was asked for and is not achievable — hints are keyed by pane id and `prunePaneSessions` reaps any whose pane is gone from every layout.
- **Found on the way, not fixed:** a pane TITLE can break its own session name. `sanitize_tmux_session_name_for_title` substitutes only `.`, `:` and whitespace, so `;`/`&`/`|`/`$` survive into `zellij attach -c {name}` — typed verbatim into cmd.exe or PowerShell. Self-inflicted, but the mundane cost bites: zellij creates a session under a different name than ymux stored, so list, kill and restore all silently miss it. Fixing it strands every existing session whose name has punctuation or Unicode, so it needs its own migration. FOLLOWUPS P1.
- **Outcome / Commits:** `bc3f013` (the lock), plus the kill/outcome commit. Nothing has run live yet — the checklist is in the plan and in PROGRESS.txt.
### 2026-08-23 — The mac build's ad-hoc path was broken from birth; hardened-runtime verification is deferred to a contributor
- **Context:** The last mac run before this session, 32421631034 on `claude/macos-universal-signing`, failed **both** matrix legs at the bundle step with `Signing with identity ""` → `The specified item could not be found in the keychain`. `main` was never affected — its copy of the workflow predates the signing block.
- **Cause — a wrong assumption, stated in a comment.** The step read "tauri falls back to the config's `-` when `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY` is empty". It does not: tauri-cli reads the variable with `env::var_os`, so **set-but-empty is `Some("")`** and overrides `"signingIdentity": "-"` in `tauri.macos.conf.json`. A YAML `env:` entry defines the variable even when its `... || ''` expression yields empty — exactly the secretless case — so tauri codesigned with an identity literally named `""`. Blank and unset are not the same thing, and the whole failure lived in that gap.
- **Decision:** the ad-hoc path `unset`s the variable in the step's shell rather than blanking it, and echoes which path it took, since blank-vs-unset is the entire bug. `0ca021e`; verified green in 32639916194 (arm64 8m37s, x64 18m12s — the first time the matrix has ever completed).
- **What that green run newly proves.** Everything downstream of bundling ran on arm64 for the first time: 274 tests, the Rule #13 embedded-frontend assert, and `lipo` on both the `.app` main executable **and** the bundled `ymux-cli` → arm64 only. The anti-Rosetta claim behind the arch split is now checked on the shipped bundle instead of asserted.
- **DEFERRED (Yossi, 2026-08-23):** the branch also flips `"hardenedRuntime": true` and adds a new `Entitlements.plist`, where mac bundles previously had neither. Under hardened runtime a missing entitlement fails at **launch**, not at build — typically a blank window with nothing in the Rust log — so CI structurally cannot catch it. No Mac was available; a contributor will verify later. Full check-list, the artifact download command, and the entitlement each symptom maps to are in FOLLOWUPS (P1, 2026-08-23). Rule #14: green here means "compiles and bundles", nothing more.
- **Merge status:** merged into `main` 2026-08-23, after `main` had moved 69 commits ahead mid-session (the 0.5.0 alignment above landed while this was being written). The merge conflicted in exactly three files — FOLLOWUPS, PROGRESS and this one — all of them append-at-the-same-anchor, resolved by keeping both sides. No code or CI file conflicted, so the mac workflow that went green is byte-identical to the one on `main`.

### 2026-08-20 — Phase 82 — the macOS port is unified into `main`; `macos-build` is retired
- **Context:** `macos-build` was a long-lived fork carrying the macOS port. By 2026-08-20 it was **65 commits behind `main` and 14 ahead** — two months of features (Phase 80 tunnel, project folders, session autoname, the sidebar pass, RTL, the YMUX rename) simply did not exist on Mac, while 14 real macOS fixes had never reached main. The drift was not an accident of this cycle; it is what a platform fork does every cycle.
- **Decision (Yossi):** merge and delete the branch. One tree, macOS behind `cfg(target_os = ...)`, so a new feature reaches Mac by default instead of in arrears. Chosen over "just update the fork" explicitly — updating it would have bought a month.
- **What the audit changed about the plan.** The problem was not symmetrical. The **transport** layer (RPC socket, tunnel, SSH agent, config dir) was already cfg-split properly and had been merged to main by earlier passes. The **local-machine** layer (shell spawn, wizards, key permissions, fonts, updater, secrets) was unconditional Windows code that *compiles* on macOS and fails at runtime — `lib.rs`, 9,680 lines, contained zero platform guards. So the merge alone was never going to produce a working Mac build, and Phase 82.D exists because of that finding.
- **`pipe_names()` is the shape to preserve, not main's single name.** macOS caps `sockaddr_un.sun_path` at 104 bytes and a long `$TMPDIR` can exceed it, with no symptom except that everything travelling the RPC socket silently never arrives. The branch handled this with a candidate list; main had gone the other way (one name, plus a legacy name for the rename shim). Resolved as: the server binds **every** candidate *and* the legacy name, the client walks the same list, and failure is only failure when none bound. Resolving toward main here would have been a silent macOS regression — the single most dangerous conflict in the merge.
- **No Keychain, deliberately.** The plaintext-password P1 was real (`dpapi_protect` returned `noprotect:<secret>` off Windows). But `save_workspace_secret` is **write-only** — there is no `dpapi_unprotect` and no loader anywhere in the tree — so a Keychain integration would have been a feature built around a value nobody consumes. Rule #2's own text sanctions the smaller answer ("otherwise keep in memory only"). Whether that store gets a reader or gets deleted is now a FOLLOWUPS P2, and it gates any future Keychain work.
- **No mac in-app updater.** Off Windows the updater downloaded the `-setup.exe`, spawned it, and exited the app 800 ms later regardless of the spawn failing — so "Install update" quit ymux and installed nothing. Both install commands now refuse up front. Mounting a `.dmg` and swapping a running `.app` is a feature, tracked rather than faked.
- **CI is the load-bearing half.** `build-macos-intel.yml` triggered only on push to `macos-build` and ran no tests. Left alone, unifying would have given macOS *less* coverage than the fork had. It now runs on push/PR to `main` with cargo test + tsc + frontend build, plus Rule #13's missing mac assertion, and stages a native `resources/ymux-cli` so the bundle finally ships a host CLI — without which the hooks/MCP transport fix would have had no binary to run.
- **Status:** green on both workflows is "compiles and passes tests", not "works". Rule #14 stands: the Keychain refusal, `chmod` key permissions, and hooks-over-Unix-socket have no test that exercises them, and need a live run on a real Mac before a release.


### 2026-08-18 — winmux → YMUX: the rebrand, executed (closes the 2026-05-27 deferral and the COMPETITIVE-SCAN caveat)
- **Context:** Yossi renamed the app to **YMUX** — "it isn't limited to Windows any more". This closes a thread flagged twice and deferred twice: `docs/COMPETITIVE-SCAN.md` §"Naming Caveat" ("8 projects named winmux on GitHub — rebrand early, before you're standing in front of a customer who says *I installed winmux and saw no SSH*"), and the 2026-05-27 entry below that ruled a rebrand out of scope because "namespace collision isn't a problem at the current scale". Scale is no longer the argument — the *name* is wrong: `win` in a product whose desktop ships for Windows and macOS and whose real work happens on remote Linux.
- **Scope — Yossi's call, of three options offered:** full rename *with* compatibility layers, over (b) full rename, clean cut, and (c) branding only. So: code, crates, file names, config dir, env vars, wire protocol, bundle identifier — everything — but nothing that an existing install or an already-provisioned remote depends on may break.
- **Also decided: history is not rewritten.** `PROGRESS.txt`, the Decided log in this file, and `.claude/worktree-audit-2026-08-03.txt` keep saying winmux, because that is what happened at the time. `docs/COMPETITIVE-SCAN.md` is untouched for a stronger reason — it is about *other people's* repos, with URLs; renaming the name inside it would turn a survey into a fiction. Everything that documents the code as it is now (README, ARCHITECTURE, CLI, CONFIG, BUILD, RELEASING, the PHASE-*-DESIGN docs) was updated, because a design doc pointing at a crate that no longer exists is a landmine.
- **Casing:** `ymux` in anything a machine reads (paths, crates, binaries, env-var stems, `productName`), `YMUX` in anything a human reads (window title, tray, i18n values, README). The i18n pass edits JSON *values* only — the keys carry identifiers (`use_ymux_tmux_config`, `DeployYmuxCliToWsl`) that had to stay.
- **The compat layers, and what each one is protecting:**
  - `%APPDATA%\winmux` → `%APPDATA%\ymux`, renamed once on first `config_dir()` call, behind a `Once` (the `log_*` calls re-enter `config_dir`; without the latch a failing rename recurses forever). Protects workspaces.json / settings.json / keys / machine-id.
  - `~/.winmux` → `~/.ymux` on remotes, merged (not `mv`-d — both can exist) by the desktop bootstrap before its hash check, and independently by the CLI at startup for hosts the bootstrap never touches. Idempotent via a `.migrated-to-ymux` marker either side leaves.
  - `WINMUX_*` env vars: the desktop, the tunnel's `last.env` and the Go daemon all write BOTH spellings; the CLI promotes `WINMUX_*` → `YMUX_*` once at startup rather than carrying thirteen per-call-site fallbacks. A pane outlives the app that opened it — a long-lived tmux session keeps whatever `set-environment -g` put there.
  - Handshake: both ends read and mirror either dialect, **but the challenge we emit is still `WINMUX-CHALLENGE`.** A pre-rename `winmux-linux-x64` does a literal `strip_prefix` and hangs up on anything else, so emitting `YMUX-` would break exactly the remotes option (a) was chosen to protect. One-line flip per side, tracked as P1 in FOLLOWUPS.
  - Also: legacy named-pipe listener, `X-Winmux-Truncated`, `use_winmux_tmux_config` serde alias, `${WINMUX_BIN}` expansion, `winmux-server`/`winmux-insights` systemd teardown, and `~/.winmux/{server,insights}` in the daemon's data-dir migration chain.
- **Two real bugs the rename surfaced, both invisible to the compiler.** `is_ymux_entry` (cli/src/hooks.rs) and the addon uninstall's Python filter both match the substring `"ymux"` — and **`"winmux"` does not contain `"ymux"`**. Left as-is, `setup-hooks` would have kept every pre-rename entry and pushed a second one beside it (two permission cards per tool call), and uninstall would have reported success while removing nothing. Both now match either spelling.
- **No shim exists for the bundle identifier.** `com.winmux.app` → `com.ymux.app` is the MSI/NSIS upgrade key; the new package installs side by side and the old one stays in Programs and Features. That is inherent to a full rename, not an oversight — it forces the release to be **0.5.0** and the release notes to carry an uninstall step. FOLLOWUPS P0.
- **Version bumps, lockstep as precedent requires (see the 2026-07-15 rebake entry):** daemon `core.Version` + `INSIGHTS_VERSION` 2.1.4 → **2.2.0** (binary, systemd unit and data dir all move — every remote needs the update offer); hooks spec `ymux_hooks_version` + `BUNDLED_CLAUDE_VERSION` 1.3.0 → **1.4.0** (commands now expand `${YMUX_BIN}` to `~/.ymux/bin/ymux`, so settings.json must re-sync).
- **Rebaked, not just renamed:** all three embedded binaries — `ymux-linux-x64` (musl) and `ymux-server-linux-{x64,arm64}` (Go cross-compile) — were rebuilt from the renamed sources. `strings` on them shows only the five deliberate `winmux` compat literals.
- **Green:** cargo check + cargo test (workspace), go vet + go test (all packages), tsc, vite build. Two new Go tests pin the dual-dialect handshake in both directions. **Not verified live — Rule #14 — see FOLLOWUPS P1 for the smoke list.**
- **Deliberately NOT touched:** `manifest.json` (describes the published v0.4.5-beta.1 assets, which really are named `winmux_0.4.5_*` — rewriting it breaks the updater for everyone on 0.4.5 today) and the GitHub repo name itself, which is Yossi's action. FOLLOWUPS P0 covers both.
### 2026-08-18 — Phase 81.G — the pane-header hook path never worked; two bugs, found by running it
- **Context:** Yossi installed 81.F and reported the pane header still showed the default name. The meta file was provably correct — `auto_name` appeared for live sessions — so the break was downstream. Two separate defects, one inherited and one mine.
- **Bug 1 (Phase 81's, not 81.F's): hook pushes identify the pane by a stale env var.** `feed.push` carries `pane_id` from the hook process's `WINMUX_PANE_ID`, inherited by the shell at spawn. On Yossi's box `tmux show-environment` returns **no per-session value for any of the 9 sessions**; only the server-global one exists, naming whichever pane connected last. So a tmux session that outlives its creating pane (or is re-attached to a different one) pushes an id the desktop no longer has, `find_workspace_for_pane` misses, and `update_pane_auto_title` **returned in total silence**. That silence is why a permanently broken path looked healthy for a month — Phase 81 shipped "compiles + unit-tested, NOT live-verified", and this is what was never verified. `claude_title` never reached a pane header either.
- **Fix 1:** `resolve_hook_pane()` — trust `pane_id` when the layout actually contains it, else map back from the `tmux_session` name the push already carries (the CLI resolves that by asking tmux, precisely because the env var is untrustworthy — `resolve_session_name`'s own comment says so). Reverse lookup via the live `pane_sessions`/`sessions` maps, covering WSL panes too (Phase 80 gave `LocalSession` a `tmux_session`). The silent no-op now logs.
- **Bug 2 (81.F's): a write-once field in a last-writer-wins store gets erased.** `session-meta.json` documents "a lost read-modify-write self-heals within one turn" — true for `claude_title`, which every `stop` rewrites; **false** for `auto_name`, written once. Observed live: an entry lost `auto_name` while its own `updated_at` never moved — the signature of a concurrent writer saving a stale snapshot.
- **Fix 2:** heal on `stop` — when `auto_name` is missing, rebuild it from the transcript's first user message. The stamp comes from **that entry's timestamp, not `now()`**: otherwise every heal would mint a new time and the "stable" name would churn, defeating the feature. Bonus: sessions that predate 81.F get their real opening line instead of a mid-conversation prompt.
- **Known cosmetic edge:** a healed name can differ by up to a minute from the original (live path stamps at first-prompt wall-clock, recovery stamps the transcript entry). One-time, display-only; not worth reading the transcript on the hot path to avoid.
- **Outcome / Commit:** branch `hook-pane-resolve` off `session-autoname`. CLI 29/29 + a live smoke that wipes `auto_name` mid-session and confirms one `stop` restores it, with the transcript's timestamp.

### 2026-08-18 — Phase 81.F — sessions get a STABLE name from their first prompt (`auto_name`)
- **Context:** Phase 81 gave sessions a display name, but sourced it from `claude_title` — the transcript summary, re-read by the `stop` hook **every turn**. Two consequences: (a) the name of a session you opened this morning silently rewrites itself as the conversation drifts, so it can't serve as an identity; (b) it carries no time anchor, so a picker full of `winmux-p_<hex>` rows tells you nothing about *when* you started each one.
- **Decision (Yossi):** derive a second, stable name — `"<two words> · <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM>"` — once, from the session's **first real prompt**, and use it as the identity in **both** the tmux picker and the pane header. Words first, timestamp second (scan by topic, disambiguate by time). `claude_title` is kept and still written every turn; it just stops being what names the session, and moves to the picker row's hover tooltip.
- **Trigger — `UserPromptSubmit`, not a new hook.** It was already registered (spec v1.3.0, for the chrome Ticker's turn timing), already carries the raw `prompt` + `session_id`, and fires in *every* permission mode — unlike `pre-tool-use`, which the CLI short-circuits under acceptEdits/bypass. So: **no hook-spec change, no `winmux_hooks_version` bump, and no `setup-hooks` re-run on existing machines.** Until now that payload's prompt text reached the CLI and was thrown away (the desktop early-returns for this subkind after stamping turn timing).
- **Two words: heuristic, not an LLM.** This hook gates the START of a turn — a `claude -p` call here would put seconds of latency in front of every first prompt, and would need a fallback for no-binary/no-network anyway. Instead: strip punctuation, drop a small bilingual filler list (he+en, matched through a leading ו/ה/ב/ל/ש/כ/מ prefix so "וגם" is caught), drop 1-char and pure-numeric tokens, take the first two survivors. Verbs are deliberately NOT fillers — "fix"/"תעדכן" are what the session is about. Nothing usable → timestamp-only name.
- **"Once per CLAUDE session", not "once per tmux session."** A pane outlives `claude`, so the meta key gets reused; the decision compares the *recorded* `claude_session_id` against the incoming one, so a second `claude` run in the same pane earns its own name. A new session that opens with a slash command (`/clear`) **clears** the stale name rather than inheriting it — the next real prompt claims it. Slash commands never name a session (the command is chrome, not the subject).
- **Design notes:** the naming decision lives in a pure `decide_auto_name()` returning the same tri-state (`None` = leave / `Some(None)` = clear / `Some(Some(n))` = set) the desktop's `update_pane_in` already uses — so the state machine is unit-testable without HOME, tmux, or a clock. The CLI returns `auto_name.or(claude_title)` as the display title under the **existing** `claude_title` push param, so old desktops keep working unchanged and the pane header simply stops churning. Rule #1 holds: the name embeds user content, lives in the meta file by design, and never reaches `hook-debug.log`.
- **Outcome / Commit:** branch `session-autoname`. CLI 23/23 unit tests + a real end-to-end smoke (first prompt names; later prompts don't; a new session id re-names; `/clear` clears; `stop` still records `claude_title`; hook log carries metadata only). The desktop half couldn't be compiled where it was written (Linux sandbox — `cargo check -p app` dies in `gdk-sys`, and installing GTK needed a blocked sudo), so **ci-windows was used as the gate instead: run 32174489370 on the branch is green** — `cargo test --workspace`, `tsc --noEmit`, frontend build, `go test`. Compiles ≠ runs (Rule #14): the live check on a real Windows box + server is still owed, see FOLLOWUPS P1. CI also flagged the expected `remote-manifest.json` sha drift — a CLI source change, so the manifest needs a real `build-linux-cli.ps1` regeneration on the release cut (FOLLOWUPS P2).

### 2026-08-18 — sha256 stays the bootstrap comparison; no version compare, no backoff
- **Context:** a previous session proposed replacing the bootstrap's sha256 check with a version comparison, and adding backoff to the "upload storm".
- **Rejected — version compare.** The desktop speaks the RPC protocol of the CLI binary it embeds, not of whatever is newer on the server. Comparing versions would let a protocol-skewed binary pass silently, which is the exact failure the same session flagged elsewhere (unattributable reverse-tunnel handshake rejections). sha256 is the correct invariant: the remote must be byte-identical to what we ship.
- **Rejected — backoff.** There is no retry loop in the bootstrap to back off; it runs once per connect, and connects are driven by the user and the reconnect driver. The right shape is a per-host lock (so concurrent panes don't race) plus a short negative cache keyed by (host, wanted sha), so a just-failed upload isn't retried on every reconnect. Implemented in `app/src-tauri/src/bootstrap_guard.rs`.
- **Accepted — skew must stay visible.** `BootstrapStatus::Skew` is now distinct from `Err`, and drives a persistent banner plus a gate on the CLI-dependent features (hooks, tickets, port-watch). The old five-second `schedule_status_clear` status line is what let a wrong-version CLI run unnoticed for a whole debugging session. The terminal and tmux are never gated — a skewed CLI must not cost the user their shell.

### 2026-07-28 — Phase 80 / 80.1 — session restore on app start + file-manager last directory
- **Context:** Yossi asked for two things: (1) every terminal should come back to the conversation it was in when the app closed; (2) the file manager should open where it was last, not at `$HOME`. He also asked for pane drag-swap — **already shipped** (`paneDrag.ts` + `workspace_swap_panes`, merged in `fb43e71`/`2ded9c4`); only drag-to-*split* ("Intent Zones", COMPETITIVE-SCAN #4.1) is still unbuilt. Scope agreed: **SSH only, for now.**
- **Why it was cheap:** the persistence was already there — closing a pane/app DETACHES tmux, never kills it (decision "DD", `001b69a`), layout + `pane_id`s live in `workspaces.json`, and the session name is deterministic. The only missing piece was WHICH session belonged to WHICH pane on the next start.
- **Options considered:** (A) new fields on the Rust `LayoutNode`/`Workspace` structs; (B) a localStorage hint map, frontend-only.
- **Decision: (B).** Same class as the floating-window rects and the sidebar width — per-machine, high-churn, disposable state; keeps Rule #7's atomic-write surface small; losing it costs one click, never data. It also kept the change frontend-only, which mattered practically: the work was done on Linux with no Rust toolchain, where `cargo check` on a Windows-targeted crate would prove nothing anyway.
- **Design points worth keeping:**
  - The stored name is the one `pane_persistence_list` reports after a successful connect — **not** a TS re-derivation of `sanitize_tmux_session_name`. Picker-chosen and title-derived names (Phase 23.I) stay correct and the naming rule stays in one place (Rust).
  - `workspace_ensure_connected` is the auth gate: headless, idempotent, agent/key-only, never auto-accepts an unknown host key. A workspace needing a password / passphrase / host-key decision simply doesn't restore — **boot never throws a prompt at the user.**
  - `pane_list_tmux_sessions` returns `Ok([])` when there's no live SSH handle (Phase 23.H), so an empty list is ambiguous and must NOT be read as "the sessions died" — hints are kept in that case, and only a name missing from a *non-empty* list is forgotten.
  - Restore waits for the pane's terminal container to be in the DOM, so `attach()`'s fit pushes real cols/rows instead of the 80×24 fallback; connects run sequentially, not N-at-once on a freshly-armed connection.
  - Panes with no remembered tmux session are deliberately left with their `[Connect]` button — a plain SSH shell leaves nothing on the server to come back to (Yossi's call).
  - Honors `auto_connect_on_workspace_select === false`: a user who turned off background SSH doesn't get network activity at boot either.
  - **80.1:** a remembered directory is only honored if it still lists; on the remote side `$HOME` answering is the tie-breaker that separates "directory gone" (move to home) from "no SSH session yet" (stay put, show the usual banner).
- **Deferred deliberately:** restoring non-active workspaces (would open an SSH channel per host at boot), restoring scrollback/screen content beyond what tmux itself replays, and local (non-SSH) panes.
- **Review pass (same day, before merge — 3 P1s, all in the new code):**
  - **The user acts during the restore loop.** It spans seconds (mount wait + sequential connects), and both up-front checks went stale in it. A pane the user connected *manually* while waiting its turn got killed and replaced (`pane_connect` kills any prior session for the pane); and `connectPane` re-resolves its workspace from `activeWs()`, so switching workspace mid-restore sent pane ids of A with workspace B — a guaranteed "no pane" error, plus `ti.workspaceId` left mistagged, which is what decides the host an OSC-8 `file://` click downloads from. **Rule taken away: a snapshot taken before an `await` is a guess afterwards.** Both are now re-checked immediately before each connect (the loop gives up entirely when the active workspace changed).
  - **80.1's persist effect raced its own mount.** It fired after `setLocalPath` but before the remote path resolved (local is a syscall, remote is an SSH round-trip), storing `{local, remote: ""}` and wiping the remembered remote directory — precisely the case the feature exists for. Fixed at both levels: the effect waits for both columns (`pathsReady`), and **`saveFmPaths` now MERGES** — an empty field means "no opinion", never "forget it".
  - **Boot must not raise a modal.** The entry above claims "boot never throws a prompt at the user", but that was only true of the *gate*: a restore connect that failed anyway (host-key change mid-boot, transient auth failure) still hit `connectPane`'s catch arms and opened a password / fingerprint dialog the user never asked for — the exact conditioning that makes click-through habitual. `ConnectOpts.restoring` now short-circuits those arms: the error lands on the pane, `[Connect]` stays.
  - Smaller: `killSession` kept forgetting the hint even when `pane_kill_session` **failed** (session may well be alive) → now only on success. `fmPaths` had no prune → `pruneFmPaths(liveWorkspaceIds)` runs alongside `prunePaneSessions`; no TTL there on purpose, the live workspace list is an exact answer. Remote directory paths carry real context about a host and shouldn't outlive their workspace.
  - **Security review — clean where it counts:** the tmux name reaches only `shell_quote`d sinks (`lib.rs:2708`, `5696`, `rpc_server.rs:1641`), FM paths reach SFTP / `fs::read_dir` with no shell, `workspace_ensure_connected` passes `accept_unknown_host=false`, no PTY content logged. One real hole closed: a `local` path from tampered localStorage was replayed at mount with no click, so `\\attacker.tld\share` would have made Windows open an outbound SMB handshake and leak a NetNTLM challenge. `fmPaths` now gates on read — local must be drive-absolute, remote POSIX-absolute. UNC is still reachable by navigating there, which is where an unattended-vs-deliberate distinction belongs.
  - **Accepted, stated plainly:** restore does one full SSH handshake **per restored pane** and re-runs the workspace `setup_command` on each, so a rate-limited / fail2ban-fronted host sees a burst of auths every time winmux opens. That is the feature working as asked, not a defect — worth knowing before it surprises someone.
  - Both new modules got unit tests (`sessionRestore.test.ts`, `fmPaths.test.ts`, 32 tests, `node:test` per the `mouseRtl.test.ts` precedent) — they're pure localStorage logic, and on a Linux box with no Windows to run they're the only verification available at all.
- **Status:** implemented on branch `feat/restore-sessions-and-fm-path`; tsc green, 32 unit tests green, **the app not run live** (Windows-only, built on Linux). Needs Yossi's live test before merge. Push to `yyhezkel/winmux` is blocked — the credential on this machine is pull-only there — so the branch exists locally plus as patches/bundle in the session scratchpad.

- **Options:** (A) pick one direction and restyle; (B) all four as selectable theme presets on the existing engine; (C) static mock screen.

### 2026-07-16 — Phase 81 — Claude-session names on tmux sessions + multi-machine visibility (server-side session-meta)
- **Context:** Yossi reported (a) the Claude session name never shows on tmux sessions in the picker — investigation showed this was never built (Phase 23.K labels were local-only and the v0.4.4-beta.2 picker didn't even read them), and (b) connecting from home "shows only home's sessions" — root cause: no filter exists; other machines' sessions arrive but display as unlabeled `winmux-p_<hex>` gibberish because labels/pane-ids live in per-machine files.
- **Decision (Yossi):** one server-side JSON — `~/.winmux/session-meta.json` — written by the Linux CLI. Name source: Claude's transcript title (last `{"type":"summary"}` line, fallback first user message), which also updates the winmux pane label (`auto_title`, manual title always wins). Visibility setting `session_visibility` = shared (default) / local, picker-only. Same Linux user across machines (confirmed), so this is purely a display/metadata problem — no shared-socket work.
- **Design notes:** no Claude rename hook exists → the `stop` hook (fires every turn) refreshes the title; cleanup is lazy-prune against `tmux ls` on every meta write (no tmux `session-closed` hook — wouldn't fire with the bundled conf disabled); origin is written by the app at session-create over SSH exec (`--origin-if-absent`, creator wins) because `tmux set-environment -g` is server-global (last-connector-wins) and unreliable for exactly the multi-machine case; labels cross the wire as hex-UTF8 (no shell quoting for Hebrew); NO tmux rename anywhere (Phase 23.J crash constraint stands); concurrency is last-writer-wins over atomic tmp+rename (stop fires every turn → self-heals).
- **Also closed in passing:** repo-root `hooks/claude-code.json` was stale at v1.1.0 (still registering SessionStart/Notification) vs the CLI's bundled v1.2.0 — synced.
- **Outcome / Commits:** Phase 81.A (CLI `session_meta.rs` + stop-hook wiring), 81.B (desktop join+filter, `machine-id`, origin/label writes), 81.C (`auto_title` on LayoutNode + rpc_server), 81.D (picker display + Settings UI + i18n), 81.E (docs + hooks spec sync). **Compiles + unit-tested; NOT yet live-verified on a real server** — needs the checklist run (meta file appears after a Claude turn, title on pane header, second-machine picker, visibility toggle, Hebrew label round-trip).

### 2026-06-18 — Phase 64 (J follow-up): Claude prints `[file]` as PLAIN TEXT, not OSC 8
- **Finding (Yossi, live Claude test):** the `OSC8 hyperlink sequence detected` diagnostic NEVER fired, and `[file] <name> (<size>)` shows as plain text with no escape sequences. So the Phase 62.B/C `linkHandler` (correct for OSC 8) has nothing to bind — Claude isn't emitting OSC 8 in winmux (TERM/capability/env, or SSH/tmux stripping). The diagnostic dlog stays in as the litmus test.
- **Two-track plan (for the dedicated J round — NOT implemented blind, both need live-Claude iteration):**
  - **Track A — make Claude emit OSC 8 (preferred; gives ABSOLUTE `file://` paths → existing linkHandler just works, no cwd problem).** Experiment first: in a pane, `export FORCE_HYPERLINK=1` (the var the common `supports-hyperlinks`/`terminal-link` libs honor) — possibly also `FORCE_HYPERLINKS=1` / a `CLAUDE_CODE_*` flag — then run `claude` and produce a file. If the `OSC8 detected` line appears, wire that env into the pane spawn automatically (typed export via the existing setup-injection, since SSH usually won't pass env vars). Confirm in docs.claude.com / anthropics/claude-code issues whether such a flag exists.
  - **Track B — regex fallback on the visible text (works always, but harder).** xterm `registerLinkProvider` matching e.g. `\[file\]\s+(\S+)\s+\(([\d.]+\s*[KMG]?B)\)` (validate against the exact live pattern). Click → download via the existing `download_remote_file_via_osc`. BLOCKER: Claude's path is relative (`.env.prod.filled`, `exports/…`) and winmux doesn't track per-pane remote cwd. A fresh SSH exec channel's `pwd` ≠ the interactive shell's cwd, so proper resolution needs OSC 7 cwd reporting (shell-integration) — a prerequisite sub-task. v1 could handle absolute paths directly + fall back to a manual-path toast (Yossi's Bonus) for relative ones.
- **Order:** J stays last per Yossi (63 → K → 63.E → L → DIFF → B1 → unzip → N → J). Track A's `FORCE_HYPERLINK` experiment is the cheapest next step and Yossi can run it manually to tell us which track to build.
- **Update (2026-07-15):** still unresolved and unscheduled. Cheapest next step unchanged: run `export FORCE_HYPERLINK=1; claude` manually in a pane and watch for the `OSC8 detected` dlog — the result decides Track A vs Track B.
- **RESOLVED data point (2026-07-15, Yossi live test): → Track B.** debug.log shows `OSC8 hyperlink sequence detected` fired 60× (recent, multiple panes) — so OSC 8 *transport* works and the linkHandler/`fileUriToPath` path is sound — but Yossi confirmed the `[file]` text has NO hover tooltip (not a link; a click only grabs selection, and zero `osc-file-link` activations ever hit the log). The detections are Claude's *other* links (https). Conclusion: Claude does not emit OSC 8 for `[file]` even with the injected env → Track A is dead.
- **Track B v1 scope (agreed):** xterm `registerLinkProvider` on the visible `[file] <name> (<size>)` pattern (validate the exact live format first); click → absolute path → existing `winmux:osc-file-link` download path; relative path → manual-path toast fallback (per-pane cwd via OSC 7 stays a separate prerequisite for full relative support). **Build gate:** `terminalInstance.ts` is currently being modified by the live font session — implement only after it lands to avoid a collision.
- **Track B v1 IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-15, same day — the font session landed and freed the file).** `FILE_LINK_RE` + `registerLinkProvider` in `terminalInstance.ts` (tolerant match: `[file] <token>` with optional `(<size>)`; size paren capped at 32 chars): absolute path (`/…`) → dispatches the existing `winmux:osc-file-link` Save-As download; relative path → new `winmux:file-link-relative` event → App copies the path to the clipboard + toast explaining it's relative to the pane's directory (i18n ×4, keys `filelink.relative.*`; parity 880). Hover shows the path as a tooltip; provider disposed in `dispose()`. One-shot `diag_log` "[file] link provider matched in pane …" (metadata only, Rule #1) so a live test separates "regex missed the real format" from "click path broken". tsc green. **Known v1 limits:** single-row matches only (a path that wraps across rows won't link); buffer-x mapping assumes no wide chars before the match on the line (true for Claude's ASCII `[file]` lines). **NEEDS YOSSI LIVE TEST:** produce a file in Claude → the `[file]` run should underline on hover; click → Save-As (absolute) or copied-path toast (relative). If the underline covers one char too many/few, the range end convention needs a ±1 — say so and it's a one-line fix.
